However, speculative decoding still suffers from a sequential dependency between drafting and verification: the draft model must wait for the target model to finish verifying tokens before generating the next speculation batch.\n\nRecent work proposes Speculative Speculative Decoding (SSD), which removes this dependency by running drafting and verification asynchronously and precomputing speculations for predicted verification outcomes. \n\npaper link: [https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.03251](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.03251)\n\nIn SSD, while the target model verifies a speculation, the draft model predicts likely verification outcomes and pre-speculates tokens for those outcomes in advance. If the actual verification result matches one of the predicted outcomes, the next speculation can be returned immediately, eliminating drafting latency. \n\nThis approach enables overlapping speculation and verification and significantly reduces idle GPU time.\n\nThe paper reports:\n\n- Up to 2× speedup over optimized speculative decoding\n\n- Up to 5× speedup over autoregressive decoding\nacross several workloads and datasets. \n\nGiven that vLLM is widely used for high-performance inference, supporting SSD could provide meaningful latency improvements.\n\n\n<div ", "created_at": "2026-04-17T14:58:12Z", "updated_at": "2026-04-17T14:58:12Z", "fetched_at": "2026-10-19T20:44:07Z", "state": null, "merged_at": null, "parent": "github:vllm-project/vllm:36037", "tags": ["speculation"], "metadata": {"author": "benchislett", "path": null, "commit_id": null}, "content_hash": "87cc85152e83a4bb932159751afcf01136d9bca82e8f62404f2b46c3237f2f3f", "excerpt_truncated": false}, {"id": "github:vllm-project/vllm:comments:4241821448", "source": "github", "kind": "comment", "url": "https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues/36037#issuecomment-4241821448", "title": "comment on vllm-project/vllm #36037", "body": "> > My thoughts:\n> > \n> > * Substantial engineering work would be required to support SSD in the vLLM framework. It would be very difficult to build this into the vLLM scheduler+worker infrastructure, a lot of the speculative decoding and kv cache management logic would need to be redesigned. A hacky prototype could put something together but this would be a very complex system to debug and maintain in the long term.\n> > * GSM8k @ concurrency=1 with TP4 is a very favorable benchmark. Using MTBench as a proxy for chat workloads would show a much steeper falloff in AL, so it would not be as valuable to run with >8 spec tokens in that scenario. Using TP1 or TP2 would also be more affordable by a large margin, the TPS would be a bit lower but the cost-effectiveness makes a huge difference in practice. This would also be exaggerated if another H100 was needed for the drafter, notably also since it no longer can fit 2 replicas in a single 8xH100 node.\n> > * Please share the actual ITL metrics you have measured for this, it is hard to tell the value in drafting so many tokens if we cannot quantify the increase in verification time.\n> > * We have some work in the pipeline to reduce drafting overhead, such as [feat(spec_decode): fuse EAGLE step slot mapping and metadata updates #33503](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/33503) as well as some open work on full-graphs and torch.compile for the draft model. There is a lot of room to improve the drafting overhead with simpler approaches.\n> \n> [@benchislett](https://github.com/benchislett) Thanks for the detailed feedback. Here is some additional experimental data for reference.\n> \n> From these numbers, I think a few points are fairly clear:\n> \n> At low concurrency, MTP drafting overhead is a large fraction of the total added latency. For example, at concurrency=1, the extra time introduced by MTP is dominated by the draft side, while verify-side growth is relatively smaller.\n> \n> At higher concurrency, both draft and verify become major limiting factors. When compute utilization is already high, speculative decoding is no longer limited by drafting overhead alone — verification overhead also becomes a major bottleneck.\n> \n> This is exactly why reducing drafting overhead alone may not fully unlock the gains at higher load. Combined with the increase in verification cost as well (more obvious at higher concurrency), this is why in my tests the overall speedup starts to decline beyond around 8~9 speculative tokens.\n\nThis makes me think that in high-acceptance-rate scenarios, autoregressive drafting methods like MTP / EAGLE-3 can themselves become a bottleneck. In that sense, parallel drafting ideas are very interesting.\n\nThat said, from my reading of the SSD paper, true overlap between drafting and verification seems to assume that the draft runner and target runner are placed on different hardware. Otherwise they would contend for the same GPU resources, which could reduce or even eliminate the overlap benefit. The issue description also frames SSD as asynchronous drafting + verification. :contentReference[oaicite:1]{index=1}\n\nSo my impression is that supporting SSD in vLLM may require quite substantial runtime / scheduler / device-placement changes, rather than being a small extension of the current speculative decoding path.\n\nCurious what the maintainers think about this.", "created_at": "2026-03-10T03:13:27Z", "updated_at": "2026-03-10T03:13:27Z", "fetched_at": "2026-10-19T20:44:07Z", "state": null, "merged_at": null, "parent": "github:vllm-project/vllm:36037", "tags": ["h100", "performance", "speculation"], "metadata": {"author": "EanWang211123", "path": null, "commit_id": null}, "content_hash": "7aea9c9c50870e34406983d10b250d33f1ce38fe6ba82db8406262b05e7d9b22", "excerpt_truncated": false}, {"id": "github:vllm-project/vllm:comments:4025959147", "source": "github", "kind": "comment", "url": "https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues/36037#issuecomment-4025959147", "title": "comment on vllm-project/vllm #36037", "body": "Thanks, that makes sense.\nI think I’ll work on some other features first, and in the meantime I can do some ablation studies on this to evaluate whether it would make a worthwhile contribution.", "created_at": "2026-03-09T18:43:46Z", "updated_at": "2026-03-09T18:43:46Z", "fetched_at": "2026-10-19T20:44:07Z", "state": null, "merged_at": null, "parent": "github:vllm-project/vllm:36037", "tags": [], "metadata": {"author": "buffett0323", "path": null, "commit_id": null}, "content_hash": "4ce2c4919fefccf2e44b0a4551e81431ca00146933c774b21e7434b0b30e8dc8", "excerpt_truncated": false}, {"id": "github:vllm-project/vllm:comments:4025824220", "source": "github", "kind": "comment", "url": "https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues/36037#issuecomment-4025824220", "title": "comment on vllm-project/vllm #36037", "body": "I'm skeptical that this project would provide a lot of value for vLLM. blockSum(float value) {\n+  __shared__ float partial[32];\n+  int lane = threadIdx.x % 32;\n+  int warp = threadIdx.x / 32;\n+#pragma unroll\n+  for (int offset = 16; offset > 0; offset /= 2)\n+ "issue", "url": "https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues/59203", "title": "[Bug]: DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash cannot run on SM120 (RTX PRO 6000): compressed-layer page_block_size=32 has no FlashInfer sparse-MLA decode kernel instantiation (only pbs=64)", "body": "### Your current environment\n\n- Hardware: 8x NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell Server Edition (SM120, 96GB), single node TP8\n- vLLM: 0.30.1rc1.dev327+g9af952c55 (nightly, pip)\n- FlashInfer: 0.7.0 (flashinfer-python + flashinfer-cubin + flashinfer-jit-cache sm120f+cu130, all three packages installed)\n- Model: deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash (official 476GiB mixed MXFP4/MXFP8 checkpoint)\n- Config: TP8, `--language-model-only`, `--engram-config '{\"cpu_offload\":true}'`, `--tokenizer-mode deepseek_v41`\n\n### 🐛 Describe the bug\n\nDeepSeek-V4.1-Flash fails to start on SM120 with:\n\n```\nValueError: SM120 sparse-MLA has no decode kernel for this shape: num_tokens=8,\nnum_heads=8, topk=128, d_qk=512, page_block_size=32, model_type=dsv4, extra_topk=0.\n```\n\n### Root cause analysis\n\nV4.1's two-tier sparse attention gives each layer a `compress_ratio` (this checkpoint: `[0,0,2,2,...,2,1,1,1,1,1]`). vLLM computes the compressed-layer KV page block size as `attn_metadata.block_size // compress_ratio`, so ratio-2 layers get pbs = 64//2 = **32**.\n\nFlashInfer's SM120 dispatch planner (`flashinfer/mla/_sparse_mla_sm120_plan.py`) hard-codes `_PAGE_BLOCK_SIZE = 64` and instantiates the dsv4 decode kernels **only** at pbs=64 (the error message itself lists `page_block_size=32` as unsupported).\n\nThere is **no single global block size that works**:\n- global block_size=64 → ratio-2 compressed layers compute pbs=32 → this crash\n- global block_size=128 → vLLM reports \"No common block size for 64 (FLASHINFER_MLA_SPARSE_DSV41: [128]; ...)\" because the SM120 kernel set has no pbs=128 dsv4 instantiation either (only 64)\n\nSo the two-tier architecture (mixed ratios 1 and 2) is fundamentally incompatible with the current SM120 kernel instantiation set, which only covers pbs=64.\n\n### Steps we already walked through to get this far (all gates passed)\n\n1. `indexer_kv_dtype=\"mxfp4\"` → rejected on SM120 (\"requires Blackwell datacenter GPUs sm_10x\"); switched to `\"fp8\"`.\n2. `indexer_sparse_logits=true` → requires MXFP4 indexer cache; removed the flag.\n3. `flashinfer_jit_cache` shim wheel (4KB) had to be installed with `--no-deps` in addition to the `sm120f` provider wheel (the provider wheel alone lacks the Python module `flashinfer_jit_cache.__version__`).\n4. torch/torchaudio CUDA mismatch from the nightly upgrade → uninstalled torchaudio.\n5. Applied #53425 (SM12x block size 64 for DeepSeek-V4) — but it only covers the `deepseek_v4` (V4) family; the `deepseek_v41` family has its own two-tier block-size chain.\n\nA debug stack trace (added at the raise site) confirms the failing path: `deepseek_v41/attention.py::forward` → `nvidia/flashinfer_sparse.py::_forward_prefill` → `flashinfer_trtllm_batch_decode_sparse_mla_dsv4` with `page_block_size=32` derived from the compressed cache tensor shape.\n\n### What is needed (either/or)\n\n1. **FlashInfer**: add SM120 dsv4 decode kernel instantiation at pbs=32 (or 32 and 128), OR\n2. **vLLM**: allow per-layer/per-cache block sizes so compressed layers can keep pbs=64 independently of the global block size.\n\nUntil then DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash is effectively unusable on SM120/SM121 hardware (the official recipe only verifies H100/H200/B200/GB200-class GPUs).\n\n### Environment details available\n\nFull `collect_env` available on request; reproduction environment is a dedicated 8x PRO 6000 node we can keep available for testing a fix.\n\nThanks!\n", "created_at": "2026-09-29T10:25:04Z", "updated_at": "2026-10-19T03:59:28Z", "fetched_at": "2026-10-19T21:00:31Z", "state": "open", "merged_at": null, "parent": null, "tags": ["b200", "h100", "deepseek-v4.1-flash", "deepseek", "mla"], "metadata": {"number": 59203, "labels": [{"id": 8746007475, "node_id": "LA_kwDOI7xefs8AAAACCU17sw", "url": "https://api.github.com/repos/vllm-project/vllm/labels/deepseek", "name": "deepseek", "color": "1995FE", "default": false, "description": "Related to DeepSeek models", "archived_at": null, "archived_by": null}, {"id": 12141724842, "node_id": "LA_kwDOI7xefs8AAAAC07QEqg", "url": "https://api.github.com/repos/vllm-project/vllm/labels/DSv4.1", "name": "DSv4.1", "color": "2c52b7", "default": false, "description": "Related to DeepSeek-V4.1 models", "archived_at": null, "archived_by": null}], "comments": 22, "state_reason": null}, "content_hash": "61526036f2737f10878b480a323239e9030a7cf47d46ae3b591afefb7e990773", "rank": -54.57347520218021, "excerpt_truncated": false}, {"id": "github:vllm-project/vllm:56771", "source": "github", "kind": "issue", "url": "https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues/56771", "title": "[Bug]: DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash + DSpark speculative decoding — illegal memory access in SM120 sparse-MLA prefill on long prompts", "body": "# [Bug]: DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash + DSpark speculative decoding — illegal memory access in SM120 sparse-MLA prefill on long prompts\n\n> Cross-ref: #55757 (same box family, same reporter — sequential sm_120 sparse-MLA investigation; that one covers the non-spec MHA prefill faults, this one is DSpark-specific).\n\n## Environment\n\n- **Model**: `deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash` (476 GiB), vision enabled\n- **Hardware**: 4× NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell (SM120, 96 GiB each), PCIe-only (no NVLink)\n- **vLLM**: nightly `69db1c26` + PR #56214 overlay (`e77daef89`), flashinfer-python 0.6.18\n- **Patches for SM120 serving**: topk 1152/640 instantiations in `sparse_mla_sm120_decode_dsv4.cu` + the Python `_DECODE_DSV4_DISPATCH` mirror, prefill dispatch arms for the same shapes (dual-cache `DISPATCH_BY_NH_PBSX_TK`), SWA cache block 32→64, divergent block sizes (ratio-1: 64, ratio-2: 128). Without DSpark this configuration serves cleanly: 60 tok/s c=1, 12K prefill in 2.1 s, stable in production.\n\n## Repro\n\nServe with:\n\n```json\n{\"method\":\"dspark\",\"num_speculative_tokens\":5,\"draft_sample_method\":\"probabilistic\",\"rejection_sample_method\":\"block\"}\n```\n\nThen send any prompt ≳4K tokens (12K reproduces reliably). Short prompts work; long prompts hard-crash the engine with an illegal memory access.\n\n## Findings (instrumented)\n\n1. `CUDA_LAUNCH_BLOCKING=1` localizes the fault to `flashinfer` `sparse_mla_sm120_prefill.cu`, `launch_prefill_mg_dual` (`cudaLaunchKernelExC`, ~line 212/268), instantiation `<DSV4, FP8, nh=16, TOPK=1152, pbs=64, pbs_x=64>`, on the chunked-prefill chunks (`ntok=4092` with k=5).\n2. **The same instantiation with the same shape tuple runs clean without DSpark** (12K prefill → 456× `(topk=1152, pbs=64, topk_x=512)`, zero errors), so it is input *content*, not kernel shape coverage.\n3. Host-side launch-time probes of all kernel inputs found per-token lengths and index values plausible — but in some runs **garbage extra-topk lengths** (`max_elen=2139095040` on a 512-wide row → kernel K-loop reads OOB) in ~9/81 calls. In other runs the crash reproduces with clean lens at launch time, so the garbage lens are a symptom, not necessarily the trigger.\n4. `compute_global_topk_indices_and_lens` (`deepseek_v4_1/common/ops/cache_utils.py`) fully initializes every lens row, so garbage lens imply never-written or overwritten memory — consistent with a cross-stream/capture-time aliasing issue specific to the DSpark path.\n5. PR #56598 (padded draft rows → generic MoE router, fixes #56389) does **not** fix this crash — tested on top of our tree. This is a different kernel (sparse-MLA prefill, not the dsv4_topk Triton router).\n\n## Suspects\n\nCross-stream memory aliasing under DSpark (freshly-allocated lens tensors consumed by asynchronously-queued prefill kernels), or the DSpark SWA index widening (`get_dspark_swa_index_width(128,5)=192`) interacting with prefill metadata that assumes window 128.\n\nHappy to provide probe For this checkpoint `n_hash_cols = (max_ngram_size - 1) * n_heads = (4 - 1) * 8 = 24`, so the assertion only holds for `tp_size <= 8` (or 12, which does not divide `num_attention_heads = 64`). Engine core init aborts.\n\nA 2-node x 8-GPU deployment is a very common shape, and there is no way to use it as a single replica for this model today.\n\n### Reproduction\n\nTwo nodes, 8x RTX PRO 6000 (SM120) each, identical vLLM venvs, ray 2.58.0 cluster spanning both:\n\n```bash\nray start --head --port=6379 --node-ip-address=<head> --num-gpus=8      # node A\nray start --address=<head>:6379 --node-ip-address=<worker> --num-gpus=8 # node B\n\nvllm serve <DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash> --tensor-parallel-size 16 \\\n  --distributed-executor-backend ray \\\n  --attention-config '{\"backend\":\"FLASHINFER_MLA_SPARSE_DSV41\",\"indexer_kv_dtype\":\"fp8\"}' \\\n  --kv-cache-dtype fp8 --engram-config '{\"cpu_offload\":true}' \\\n  --max-model-len 131072 --gpu-memory-utilization 0.92 --language-model-only\n```\n\nThe ray placement group is created fine (16/16 GPUs reserved), weights start loading, then:\n\n```\nFile \"vllm/models/deepseek_v41/nvidia/engram.py\", line 302, in __init__\n    super().__init__(num_embeddings, dim, head_sizes, block_size)\nFile \"vllm/models/deepseek_v41/common/engram.py\", line 668, in __init__\n    assert (num_shards - 1) * self.part_n_hash_cols < self.n_hash_cols, (\nAssertionError: Engram sharding leaves ranks without hash heads: 24 heads over 16 shards\n```\n\nThe same command with `--tensor-parallel-size 8` on one node works (we have been serving this model with TP8 successfully, including GSM8K 99/100).\n\n### Analysis\n\n`vllm/models/deepseek_v41/common/engram.py`:\n\n```python\nself.n_hash_cols = len(head_sizes)                      # 24 for this checkpoint\nself.tp_size = get_tensor_model_parallel_world_size()\nnum_shards, head_rank = self._get_shard_info()          # -> (self.tp_size, tp_rank)\nself.part_n_hash_cols = triton.cdiv(self.n_hash_cols, num_shards)\n# TODO: Support row-wise sharding when there are too few hash heads.\nassert (num_shards - 1) * self.part_n_hash_cols < self.n_hash_cols, (...)\n```\n\n`_get_shard_info()` returns `(self.tp_size, get_tensor_model_parallel_rank())` unconditionally - the sharding degree is hard-wired to the TP degree, and neither `EngramConfig` (`cpu_offload` / `embedding_across_dp` / `dp_shared_memory` / `use_thp`) nor any env var changes it.\n\n`n_hash_cols = (max_ngram_size - 1) * n_heads = 3 * 8 = 24` is fixed by the checkpoint's `text_config`. Evaluating the assertion for all TP degrees that divide `num_attention_heads = 64`:\n\n| tp_size | `ceil(24/tp)` | `(tp-1) * ceil(24/tp)` | `< 24` |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| 1 | 24 | 0 | yes |\n| 2 | 12 | 12 | yes |\n| 4 | 6 | 18 | yes |\n| 8 | 3 | 21 | yes |\n| 16 | 2 | 30 | **no** |\n| 32 | 1 | 31 | **no** |\n\nSo the only usable degrees for this model are 1/2/4/8; 16 GPUs cannot form one replica.\n\n### What is *not* the problem\n\nWorth noting because it rules out the usual suspects for multi-node TP:\n\n- **Kernels**: at TP16 the attention heads per rank become 4, which `_pad_to_supported_q_heads` pads up to 8, i.e. exactly the same `flashinfer.mla._sparse_mla_sm120._DECODE_DSV4_DISPATCH` entry `(8, 512)` used at TP8. We verified the dispatch check passes before launching.\n- **Interconnect**: we benchmarked the 2-node fabric first (8x ConnectX-7 400G NDR per node, GDR enabled): 16-rank cross-node `all_reduce_perf` gives 39.5us @ 8 KB and 42.3us @ 32 KB, i.e. essentially the same per-collective latency as the single-node 8-rank collective we measured inside the decode loop with the torch profiler (43.8us for a 10 KB payload). So a small-message cross-node collective is not an obstacle here; the Engram assertion is the *only* thing blocking TP16.\n\n### Expected behavior / suggestion\n\nEither\n1. implement the TODO (`row-wise sharding when there are too few hash heads`) so that `n_hash_cols < tp_size` is handled, or\n2. at minimum make the sharding degree configurable (e.g. shard over a sub-group of the TP group / over DP) and/or fail with an actionable message that names the constraint (`n_hash_cols` vs `tp_size`) instead of a bare assertion.\n\nA `ValueError` early in config validation would also be friendlier than aborting during model init after the placement group and weight loading have been set up.\n\n### Environment\n\n- vLLM 0.30.1rc1.dev382+g09c47db1c (nightly, uv), PyTorch 2.13.0+cu132, FlashInfer 0.7.1 (built from main), DeepGEMM 2.8.0+6901431 (main)\n- 2 nodes x 8x NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell Server Edition (96 GB, SM120), driver 590.48.01, CUDA 13\n- ray 2.58.0, `VLLM_USE_RAY_V2_EXECUTOR_BACKEND=0`\n- Model: DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash (`max_ngram_size` 4, `engram_n_heads` 8, `num_attention_heads` 64)\n\nHappy to test a patch - we still have the 2-node ray cluster standing and can re-run immediately. "github:vllm-project/vllm:51742", "source": "github", "kind": "pr", "url": "https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51742", "title": "[Spec Decode] Fix KV cache group explosion for hybrid models", "body": "## Purpose\r\n\r\nWhen profiling Qwen/Qwen3.6-35B-A3B + z-lab/Qwen3.6-35B-A3B-DFlash, we found the KV cache group_size become 1. This increases build metadata overhead.\r\n\r\nWe found the target model Qwen/Qwen3.6-35B-A3B layer buckets are [30 mamba, 10 full]. The DFlash drafter z-lab/Qwen3.6-35B-A3B-DFlash adds [5 sliding, 1 full], the added 1 full attention layer that can't merge with the target's 10 full attention layers. The current logic used `min` as the group size [here](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/blob/v0.27.0/vllm/v1/core/kv_cache_utils.py#L1245-L1246).\r\n\r\n`group_size = min([30, 10, 5, 1]) = 1` makes it to split into 46 groups of 1 layer each.\r\n\r\nSo this PR update `_get_kv_cache_groups_uniform_page_size` to exclude buckets with 1 layer from the `min` computation.\r\n\r\nResult: layers [30 mamba, 10 full, 5 sliding, 1 full] become 10 groups instead of 46 groups. e2e output throughput improves 102% at batch size 1 on H200.\r\n\r\n```\r\nGroup 0: MambaSpec, layers=5\r\nGroup 1: MambaSpec, layers=5\r\nGroup 2: MambaSpec, layers=5\r\nGroup 3: MambaSpec, layers=5\r\nGroup 4: MambaSpec, layers=5\r\nGroup 5: MambaSpec, layers=5\r\nGroup 6: FullAttentionSpec, layers=5\r\nGroup 7: FullAttentionSpec, layers=5\r\nGroup 8: SlidingWindowSpec, layers=5\r\nGroup 9: FullAttentionSpec, layers=1\r\n```\r\n\r\nNote: This PR does not cause regression for other models.\r\n\r\n```\r\n  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────┐\r\n  │                  Model                  │          Before           │           After            │\r\n  ├─────────────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤\r\n  │ Qwen3.6-35B-A3B                         │ 4 groups (10 layers each) │ 4 groups (unchanged)       │\r\n  ├─────────────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤\r\n  │ Qwen3.6-35B-A3B + DFlash                │ 46 groups (1 layer each)  │ 10 groups                  │\r\n  ├─────────────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤\r\n  │ Qwen3.6-35B-A3B + Eagle                 │ 41 groups (1 layer each)  │ 5 groups                   │\r\n  ├─────────────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤\r\n  │ gemma-4-26B-A4B-it (5:1 sw/full)        │ 6 groups                  │ 6 groups (unchanged)       │\r\n  ├─────────────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤\r\n  │ gpt-oss-20b (12 sw + 13 full)           │ 2 groups                  │ 2 groups (unchanged)       │\r\n  └─────────────────────────────────────────┴───────────────────────────┴────────────────────────────┘\r\n```\r\n\r\n## Test Plan\r\n\r\n```\r\npytest -s -v tests/v1/core/test_kv_cache_utils.py\r\n```\r\n## Test Result\r\n\r\nUnit tests passed.\r\n\r\n## Profiling\r\n\r\nProfiled Qwen/Qwen3.6-35B-A3B + GDNAttentionMetadataBuilder.build() takes a long time (~900µs), because the batch level GDN spec decode metadata (masks, decode/prefill/spec split, token indices, query_start_loc cumsums) is computed repeatedly in each group build(). This compute can be moved one level up out of each group build().\r\n\r\n* Compute the batch level GDN spec decode metadata once per step in `compute_spec_metadata` and store it on model_specific_attn_metadata, instead of recomputing it inside each GDN group's build(). Only the block_table dependent state index gathers stay per-group in build().\r\n* e2e output throughput improves 61% at batch size 1,  12% at batch size 16 with DFlash (tested on H200).\r\n\r\n## "github:vllm-project/vllm:35714", "source": "github", "kind": "pr", "url": "https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/35714", "title": "Support multimodal speculative decoding in draft_model mode", "body": "## Purpose\r\nThis PR follows the needs discussed in the following issues and adds support for multimodal speculative decoding in vLLM’s `draft_model` (**both parallel and non-parallel draft mode**) mode:\r\n\r\n* [https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues/28947](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues/28947)\r\n* [https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues/33458](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues/33458)\r\n\r\nCurrently, vLLM does not support speculative decoding for multimodal models in `draft_model` mode. This PR adds support for the following scenarios:\r\n\r\n1. **Target model + draft model are both multimodal models (VLMs)**\r\n   Example: `Qwen2.5-VL-32B-Instruct + Qwen2.5-VL-3B`\r\n\r\n2. **Cross-modal speculative decoding** (target model is VLM, draft model is text-only)\r\n   Example: `Qwen2.5-VL-32B-Instruct + Qwen3-0.6B`  `Qwen3-VL-8B-Instruct + amdPARD-Qwen3-0.6B`\r\n\r\nThe cross-modal adaptation is implemented with reference to the speculative decoding design in **SGLang standalone mode**.\r\n\r\n---\r\n\r\n## Background and Problem Statement\r\n\r\nThere are several key issues in the current vLLM `draft_model` path for multimodal scenarios:\r\n\r\n* Multimodal support is determined using the **target model config**, which causes misclassification in the “VLM target + text draft” case\r\n* M-RoPE / positional encoding cache and kernel position handling are incorrect in some paths, which can lead to:\r\n\r\n  * startup failures (`NotImplementedError` / `AttributeError`)\r\n  * position corruption under concurrency\r\n  * reduced acceptance rate\r\n* When both target and draft are VLMs (both using M-RoPE), the 3D M-RoPE positions are not preserved correctly\r\n* Heterogeneous vocab-size combinations are overly restricted, blocking some practically safe combinations (e.g., using `Qwen3-0.6B` as draft for `Qwen2.5-VL-32B`)\r\n\r\nThis PR focuses on **correctness and functionality enablement**, so that multimodal / cross-modal speculative decoding can run correctly in `draft_model` mode.\r\n\r\n---\r\n\r\n## Changes\r\n\r\n### 1. `eagle.py` (6 changes)\r\n\r\n* **Use draft model config (instead of target model config) to determine `supports_mm_inputs`**\r\n  Avoids misclassifying the draft model as multimodal in the `VLM target + text draft` scenario and entering the wrong code path.\r\n\r\n* **Always initialize `self.positions`**\r\n  Fixes potential `AttributeError` when the Triton kernel writes position buffers in the M-RoPE draft-model path.\r\n\r\n* **Fix incorrect broadcasting in `_set_positions` under concurrency**\r\n  Prevents 1D `positions` from being accidentally reduced to a scalar and broadcasting request-0’s position to the entire batch.\r\n\r\n* **Fix `kernel_positions` computation in `set_inputs_first_pass` for cross-modal cases**\r\n  In the `target(M-RoPE) + draft(1D RoPE)` scenario, computes the real sequential positions instead of using compressed M-RoPE time positions, preventing draft position / KV-cache misalignment.\r\n\r\n* **Fix `mrope_positions` filling for dual-VLM (dual M-RoPE) scenarios**\r\n  Directly copies the target’s 3D M-RoPE positions for input tokens, avoiding corruption of image-token H/W dimensions caused by kernel-written 1D positions.\r\n\r\n* **Complete positional-encoding compatibility paths for multimodal / cross-modal speculative decoding**\r\n  Unifies position propagation and cache alignment logic across M-RoPE and 1D RoPE combinations for target/draft pairs.\r\n\r\n### 2. `speculative.py` (1 change)\r\n\r\n* **Relax heterogeneous vocab validation**\r\n  Only raises when `draft_vocab > target_vocab` (possible out-of-bounds risk); allows safe combinations where `draft_vocab < target_vocab` (may slightly reduce acceptance rate).\r\n\r\n---\r\n\r\n## Summary: Supported Scenarios\r\n\r\n| Scenario                 | Before                                                     | After                                          |\r\n| ------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------- |\r\n| VLM target + text draft  | Startup error `NotImplementedError`                        | ✅ Works, positional encoding aligned correctly |\r\n| VLM target + VLM draft   | Startup error `AttributeError`, incorrect M-RoPE positions | ✅ Works, full 3D M-RoPE preserved              |\r\n| Text target + text draft | Already worked                                             | ✅ Unaffected                                   |\r\n\r\n---\r\n## Test Plan\r\n\r\nDataset: MMStar\r\nEnvironment: 4090D\r\nTarget model: `Qwen2.5-VL-32B-Instruct`\r\nDraft \\\r\n-tp 4 --max-model-len 10240 \\\r\n--gpu-memory-utilization 0.9  --max-cudagraph-capture-size 32 \\\r\n--speculative_config '{\"model\": \"/models/Qwen3-0.6B/\", \"num_speculative_tokens\": 3, \"method\": \"draft_model\"}'\r\n```\r\n\r\n```bash\r\nCUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=4,5,6,7 \\\r\nvllm serve /models/Qwen2.5-VL-32B-Instruct/ \\\r\n--host 0.0.0.0 \\\r\n--port 30000 \\\r\n-tp 4 "github:vllm-project/vllm:61311", "source": "github", "kind": "pr", "url": "https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61311", "title": "[Core] Shared abstractions for specialized decode kernels", "body": "_(the text below is written by myself)_\r\n\r\nThis is an effort to (1) increase standardization and (2) limit LoC changes in model files as a result from adding numerous specialized decode kernels (_aka \"monokernels\"_). \r\n\r\nThe kernels themselves will naturally remain difficult to review thoroughly, but ideally model code should remain easy to review. The abstraction layer should deliberately be made as thin as possible,\r\n\r\nHeavily inspired by https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61867, and https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues/60904.\r\n\r\n**Note: this PR drops `--monokernel` in favor of `--specialized-decode`**\r\n\r\n# 1. Goal\r\n\r\n**An author of a specialized decode kernel should write:**\r\n\r\n1. A FlyDSL/CuTeDSL implementation and its utilities, in `vllm/models/<model>/<vendor>/`\r\n2. The conditions under which it can run\r\n3. Minimal glue connecting it to the model code\r\n\r\n**The author should not have to write:**\r\n\r\n1. Another new way to glue the kernel into the model code\r\n3. Basic config checks during start up, re-used by many specialized kernels needs anyway (parallelism, spec decode, graph mode)\r\n4. Checks of whether to run the specizlied kernel or regular vllm code at every step\r\n5. TP rank agreement of the above\r\n6. Updating of CUDA graph capture sizes\r\n7. Kernel selection when one model has more than one specialized kernel\r\n8. Optional: JIT/warm-up registration\r\n\r\n\r\nThis PR implements the latter points.\r\n\r\n# 2. How\r\n\r\nDeclare when the specialized kernel can run:\r\n```python\r\n# refuse causes crash, skip causes fallback to regular vllm execution\r\n\r\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\r\nclass DecodeSpec(Generic[KernelT]):\r\n    name: str\r\n    supports: frozenset[Feature]\r\n    widths: Callable[[VllmConfig], tuple[int, ...]]\r\n    refuse_config: Callable[[VllmConfig], list[str]]\r\n    refuse_model: Callable[[Any], list[str]]\r\n    refuse_caches: Callable[[VllmConfig, KernelT, Mapping[str, Any]], list[str]]\r\n    skip_step: Callable[[VllmConfig, KernelT, int], str | None]\r\n    build: Callable[[VllmConfig, Any, tuple[int, ...]], KernelT]\r\n```\r\n\r\n`KernelT` is a stand-in for the entire model forward, implementing\r\n\r\n```python\r\nclass DecodeImpl(Protocol):\r\n    cache_layers: tuple[str, ...]\r\n\r\n    def run(self, step: Step, input_ids, inputs_embeds, **kwargs) -> Any: ...\r\n    def bind_caches(self, caches: Mapping[str, Any]) -> None: ...\r\n```\r\n\r\n**Optional:** A kernel that compiles its launches from keys (one per width, per layer plan, or whatever the kernel keys on) can additionally implement\r\n\r\n```python\r\n@runtime_checkable\r\nclass DecodeWarmable(Protocol):\r\n    def warmup_keys(self) -> list[Any]: ...\r\n    def compile(self, key: Any) -> None: ...\r\n```\r\n\r\nwhich will cause the dispatcher to auto-register it for warmup. The existing DSv41 implementation uses this, and so would https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60889, https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60731, and https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60683. The Kimi K3 nvidia kernel jits eagerly since its specialized decode kernel is not created in a region with an active jit registry; hence, this feature is optional.\r\n\r\nOnce the kernel author has written their CuteDSL/FlyDSL kernel, the two remaining points\r\n\r\n> 2. The conditions under which it can run\r\n> 3. Minimal glue connecting it to the model code\r\n\r\nis implemented through a Spec and wiring the kernel code through a DecodeImpl. The remainder is taken place by the shared `SpecializedDecode` utility.\r\n\r\n_Note: we deliberately put no further constraint on how one implements `run`. There is a big benefit letting each model decide exactly how to structure this. We've already seen many different ways of structuring the underlying flydsl/cutedsl kernels._\r\n\r\n## 2.1 Model-side code\r\n\r\nThe model-side code will look like this:\r\n\r\n```python\r\nclass MyForCausalLM(nn.Module):\r\n    def __init__(self, *, vllm_config, prefix=\"\"):\r\n        self.decode_spec = select_decode_spec(vllm_config, SPECS)\r\n        self.specialized_decode: SpecializedDecode[MyKernel] | None = None\r\n\r\n    def process_weights_after_loading(self):\r\n        # optionally: this code can also be placed in the model state init, but then you cannot benefit from auto-registration of warmup\r\n        if self.decode_spec is not None:\r\n            self.specialized_decode = SpecializedDecode(\r\n                self.model, self.vllm_config, self.decode_spec\r\n            )\r\n\r\n    def forward(self, input_ids, positions, intermediate_tensors=None, inputs_embeds=None, **kwargs):\r\n        decode = self.specialized_decode\r\n        if decode is not None:\r\n            step = decode.plan(positions, intermediate_tensors)\r\n            if step is not None:\r\n                return decode.run(step, input_ids, inputs_embeds, **kwargs)\r\n        return self.model(input_ids, positions, intermediate_tensors, inputs_embeds, **kwargs)\r\n```\r\n\r\nplus one hook to prepare the kv cache\r\n\r\n```python\r\nclass MyModelState(DefaultModelState):\r\n    def initialize_kv_cache(self, kv_cache_config, block_tables):\r\n        super().initialize_kv_cache(kv_cache_config, block_tables)\r\n        if self.model.specialized_decode is not None:\r\n            self.model.specialized_decode.bind_caches()\r\n```\r\n\r\nOn the model side nothing else needs to be touched. \r\n\r\n# 3. How to: adding a second kernel for the same model\r\n\r\nSay DSv4.1 gets a gfx942 kernel next to the gfx950 one. Write the FlyDSL kernel, then:\r\n\r\n```python\r\n# vllm/models/deepseek_v41/amd/specialized_decode.py\r\n\r\nclass DSV41Gfx942Kernel(DSV41Kernel):           # reuse what you want\r\n    def run(self, step: Step, input_ids, inputs_embeds, **kwargs) -> Any: ...\r\n        ...\r\n        return self.runner.run_gfx942_kernel(...)\r\n\r\n    def compile(self, key):\r\n        width, idx = key\r\n        self.runner.kernels(width, self.plans[idx])\r\n\r\n\r\ndef _refuse_config_gfx942(vllm_config) -> list[str]:\r\n    reasons = []\r\n    if not current_platform.is_gfx942():\r\n        reasons.append(\"needs a gfx942 GPU\")\r\n    ...\r\n    return reasons\r\n\r\n\r\nDECODE_SPEC_GFX942 = DecodeSpec[DSV41Gfx942Kernel](\r\n    name=\"dsv41-cdna3\",\r\n    supports=frozenset({Feature.PIPELINE_PARALLEL}),   # no spec decode here\r\n    widths=lambda cfg: tuple(range(1, 17)),            # fewer rows than gfx950\r\n    refuse_config=_refuse_config_gfx942,\r\n    refuse_model=_refuse_model,                        # shared with gfx950\r\n    refuse_caches=_refuse_caches,\r\n    skip_step=_skip_step,\r\n    build=DSV41Gfx942Kernel,\r\n)\r\n\r\nSPECS = (DECODE_SPEC_GFX950, DECODE_SPEC_GFX942)\r\n```\r\n\r\nThen one line in the model:\r\n\r\n```python\r\nself.decode_spec = select_decode_spec(vllm_config, SPECS)\r\n```\r\n\r\nThe dispatcher takes the first compatible spec. Specializing on something other than arch works the same way. A hyper-specialized batch-size 1 kernel can just set `widths= lambda cfg: (1,)` and be placed first in the `SPECS` list.\r\n\r\n# 4. How to: a kernel that only covers part of a layer\r\n\r\nA specialized decode kernel might cover only part of a layer, e.g. only o-proj through post-moe AR.\r\n\r", "created_at": "url": "https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/39165", "title": "[Bugfix] Fix pipeline load imbalance in scheduler", "body": "\r\n\r\n## Purpose\r\nIn the current pipeline parallel implementation (non-Ray V2), the scheduler allows the first stage to greedily schedule requests up to the global max_num_seqs limit. It does not account for the capacity of downstream pipeline stages. This leads to severe load imbalance where the first stage becomes a bottleneck, causing downstream stages to starve or forcing the pipeline to process requests in inefficient \"stop-and-wait\" bursts.\r\nThis PR fixes the imbalance by introducing a constraint on the number of requests scheduled in the \"pre-pipeline\" phase. We calculate a fair share of requests per pipeline stage (max_num_pre_pipeline_reqs) and enforce this limit during the scheduling of running, new, and resumed requests.\r\n\r\n**Examples:**\r\n1. Underutilization (Small Batch): With max_num_seqs=32 and pipeline_parallel_size=2, if a user sends 16 requests, the scheduler might try to push all 16 into the first stage. This leaves the second stage idle initially and creates a bursty workload. Ideally, the load should be balanced (8 requests per stage) to keep the pipeline full.\r\n2. Imbalance (Large Batch): With max_num_seqs=32 and pipeline_parallel_size=2, if the system is under heavy load (e.g., 32 requests), the first stage might grab a disproportionate share (e.g., 18 requests) while the second stage handles fewer (14 requests). Since the pipeline speed is limited by the slowest stage, the stage with 18 requests becomes a straggler, increasing overall latency.\r\n\r\n## Test Plan\r\nQwen3-32B  PP=2\r\n--random-input-len 1024 the run then crashes (Xid + CUBLAS_STATUS_EXECUTION_FAILED)", "body": "## Summary\n\nIn `CustomAllreduce` (`vllm/distributed/device_communicators/custom_all_reduce.py`), the NVLink-multicast (\"multimem\") reduce-scatter buffer is not created at startup. It is created the first time a reduce-scatter of a particular size happens, which can be many minutes into serving. On our 8× B300 node, the two runs that took this path both crashed right after the buffer was created. The many runs that never took it ran clean.\n\n## Background\n\nOn NVLink-connected GPUs (MNNVL), custom all-reduce can run reduce-scatter on a low-SM \"multimem\" kernel. That kernel needs a dedicated multicast buffer: device memory mapped into an NVLink multicast object shared by all ranks. The other MNNVL buffers are set up when the communicator is constructed. This one is not.\n\n## What the code does\n\n1. `_select_reduce_scatter_backend` (`custom_all_reduce.py:621` on main at `242e4213f`) picks the `mnnvl_multimem` backend only when the reduce-scatter input is **larger than 16 MiB and at most 64 MiB**. These are `_DEFAULT_MNNVL_REDUCE_SCATTER_MAX_SIZE` and `_DEFAULT_MNNVL_MULTIMEM_REDUCE_SCATTER_MAX_SIZE` (lines 132–133).\n2. The first time it picks that backend, it calls `_init_mnnvl_multimem_reduce_scatter_buffer()` (line 674). That allocates and maps the multicast buffer **in the middle of serving**, while other kernels and CUDA graphs are running.\n3. It then logs `Using the low-SM MNNVL multimem reduce-scatter kernel.` (line 682).\n\nWhether a run ever lands in the 16–64 MiB window depends on how many tokens are batched at runtime. So the same deployment can hit this path in one run and not in another. The two thresholds are hard-coded defaults; there is no setting to change them.\n\n## What we observed\n\n- **Setup:** 8× NVIDIA B300 (sm_103), one node with NVSwitch. NCCL 2.30.7, torch 2.13.0+cu130. Kimi-K3 with TP=8 + expert parallel + decode-context-parallel 8, MegaMoE, CUDA graphs on.\n- **Run 1:** the log line above appeared 13–14 minutes into the run. In the same second the GPU raised an NVML Xid error and cuBLAS failed with `CUBLAS_STATUS_EXECUTION_FAILED`, and the engine died.\n- **Run 2:** same log line at the same point, then `EngineDeadError`.\n- **Every other run on the same GPUs** (dozens, same config family) never logged that line and finished cleanly. ECC errors stayed at 0 and there were no row remaps, so a faulty GPU is unlikely.\n\nWe have not proven causation. The lazy buffer creation is the only code path unique to the two failing runs, and it ran immediately before the fault.\n\n## Suggested fix\n\nDo either of these:\n- Create the multimem reduce-scatter buffer at communicator setup, like the other MNNVL buffers, so it never happens during serving.\n- Make the two size thresholds configurable, so a deployment can stay out of the 16–64 MiB window without disabling custom collectives altogether.\n", "created_at": "2026-10-12T07:05:02Z", "updated_at": "2026-10-12T07:44:54Z", "fetched_at": "2026-10-19T20:56:40Z", "state": "open", "merged_at": But above **256 KB** the *unfused* all-reduce uses vLLM's fast `multimem_all_reduce` (NVLink multicast), which is faster than flashinfer's one-shot AR. So in the **256 KB–512 KB** band the pass trades the fast `multimem` AR for a slower fused AR — a net regression:\n- **−1.9 to −4.3 µs/op** (microbench, CUDA-graph timed)\n- **−3.4 % / −7.4 % decode throughput at batch 96 / 128** (Qwen3-30B-A3B, TP8)\n\nFix: cap the fusion at the size where the fast unfused AR takes over (256 KB here), gated on multimem availability.\n\n## Environment\n8×H200 (sm_90), NVLink/NVSwitch, TP8, bf16. vLLM `0.1.dev1916`, flashinfer `0.6.11.post2`. Defaults `VLLM_ALLREDUCE_USE_SYMM_MEM=1`, `VLLM_USE_NCCL_SYMM_MEM=0`, `VLLM_ALLREDUCE_USE_FLASHINFER=0`.\n\n## Root cause\n`cuda_communicator.all_reduce` sends the unfused AR to vLLM's custom one-shot kernel below `CUSTOM_ALL_REDUCE_MAX_SIZES[\"9.0\"][8] = 262144 B` and to `multimem_all_reduce` at/above it (the NCCL-symm-mem and flashinfer-regular-AR branches are off by default). `AllReduceFusionPass` emits flashinfer's one-shot trtllm AR+RMSNorm for any message below `FI_ALLREDUCE_FUSION_MAX_SIZE_MB[90][8] = 0.5 MB`. So in 256 KB–512 KB the pass replaces the fast `multimem` AR with the slower flashinfer one-shot; the ~2 µs RMSNorm saving doesn't offset flashinfer being ~2× slower than `multimem` there.\n\n## Evidence\n**1. Per-op** (microbench, CUDA-graph timed, default config, H=2048). `delta = unfused − fused` µs/op; `>0` = fusion wins:\n\n| tokens | bytes | fused | unfused (AR backend) | delta |\n|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|\n| 48 | 192 KB | 10.4 | 11.2 (custom) | +0.86 |\n| 64 | 256 KB | 12.1 | 12.1 (multimem) | +0.05 |\n| 96 | 384 KB | 15.0 | 13.1 (multimem) | −1.87 |\n| 128 | 512 KB | 17.9 | 13.6 (multimem) | −4.30 |\n\nCrossover is byte-governed, constant at 256 KB across hidden sizes 2048/4096/7168 (token thresholds 64/32/18).\n\n**2. The regression is specifically `multimem`.** With `VLLM_ALLREDUCE_USE_SYMM_MEM=0` (unfused AR stays custom one-shot), the 256–384 KB band flips back to a win (N=64 +3.79, N=96 +1.48 µs). (A small −1 µs residual remains only at 512 KB.)\n\n**3. End-to-end** (decode throughput, cap 0.5 vs 0.25 MB; median of 6, 256 steps):\n\n| batch | cap=0.5 | cap=0.25 | Δ |\n|---:|---:|---:|---:|\n| 64 | 10584 | 10655 | +0.7 % (noise) |\n| 96 | 14421 | 14912 | +3.4 % |\n| 128 | 17425 | 18713 | +7.4 % |\n\nLocalized to the band (≈0 at batch ≤64), well outside per-batch noise (~0.2–0.3 %).\n\n## Proposed fix\nCap the fusion at the active fast-AR threshold: `min(current_cap, CUSTOM_ALL_REDUCE_MAX_SIZES[\"9.0\"][8])` when multimem is on (or the ~128 KiB NCCL-symm-mem bound when `VLLM_USE_NCCL_SYMM_MEM=1`), gated on availability; unchanged on non-multimem nodes (fusion wins to ~384 KB there). Keep byte/MB-shaped. Thresholds are config-overridable (#23722). Precedent: #42409, #37756.\n\n## Config caveat\nWith the non-default `VLLM_USE_NCCL_SYMM_MEM=1`, NCCL-symm-mem wins from ~128 KiB, moving the crossover lower — hence keying the cap to the active fast-AR threshold rather than a fixed value.\n\n### Report of performance regression\n\n_No response_\n\n### Misc discussion on performance\n\n_No response_\n\n### Your current environment (if you Purpose\r\n\r\nAdd `hybrid_ep` as a new `--all2all-backend` option for MoE expert parallel deployments, addressing a request from the Nemotron inference team. Freely inspired by the TorchTitan HybridEP integration (pytorch/torchtitan#2207).\r\n\r\nKey differences from `deepep_high_throughput`:\r\n\r\n- Uses `HybridEPBuffer` (fused permute+communication kernels) instead of `Buffer` (separate steps)\r\n- NVLink-only mode -- works on any NVLink-connected topology (NVL8, NVL72, etc.)\r\n- Supports CUDA graph capture -- during CG capture/replay, the dispatch handle from warmup is reused, bypassing the CG-incompatible permute_preprocessing path\r\n- Dedicated `HybridEPPrepareAndFinalize` class with its own code path\r\n\r\nThis PR includes false}, {"id": "github:vllm-project/vllm:50505", "source": "github", "kind": "pr", "url": "https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50505", "title": "[Core][Distributed] Size custom all-reduce buffers at init so batch-invariant mode can use them", "body": "### Purpose\n\nFixes #50136.\n\n`CustomAllreduce.should_custom_ar()` decides per call whether to use the custom\nall-reduce or fall through to NCCL, based on `inp_size < self.max_size`. The\nall-reduce tensor is `[num_tokens, hidden_size]`, so that decision tracks the\ntoken count of the forward pass — i.e. batch composition — and the same request\ncan take different reduction orders in different batches. Under\n`VLLM_BATCH_INVARIANT=1` that is a correctness bug, which is why\n`ParallelConfig` currently forces `disable_custom_all_reduce = True` and every\nbatch-invariant TP run pays the pinned-NCCL path.\n\nThis removes the size dependency instead of the whole code path.\n\n**Which review this is, settled.** The open question was whether the intended guarantee is *per request* (a request is bitwise reproducible under any batching) or *per engine* (a given engine configuration is internally consistent) — a bug under the first reading, a documented limitation under the second. @tolleybot answered it on this thread: per-request is what the production case needs, since regression tests and trusted production output require a prompt to give bitwise-identical logprobs however it is batched, across runs and restarts. This PR is therefore a fix, not a feature.\n\n### What changed\n\n**1. Decide once, at init** (`custom_all_reduce.py`). Under\n`VLLM_BATCH_INVARIANT`, derive the largest all-reduce the engine can ever issue\nfrom `max_num_batched_tokens × hidden_size × dtype_size` and size `max_size` —\nand therefore the IPC buffers — to cover it. The gate can then never flip\nmid-run. If the requirement exceeds the cap, or the config cannot be read,\ncustom all-reduce declines at init and logs why, rather than disabling itself\nsilently.\n\n**2. Fail loud, never fall back silently** (`should_custom_ar`). If an\nall-reduce still exceeds the buffer, raise instead of falling through to NCCL.\nThis is the load-bearing design decision. A silent fallback is precisely the\nmechanism in #50136; a fix whose failure mode reproduces the bug it fixes would\nbe worse than no fix. If the bound is ever wrong — an unmodelled path, a padded\nwidth I did not account for — the run stops with a message naming the tensor\nsize and the buffer, instead of quietly producing non-reproducible logprobs.\n\n**3. Pin the kernel** (`csrc/custom_all_reduce.cuh`). Force\n`cross_device_reduce_1stage` for all sizes under batch invariance, and hard\nerror on an explicit `VLLM_CUSTOM_ALLREDUCE_ALGO=2stage`. The 1-stage kernel\nsums over absolute rank indices with no address reordering (its own comment\nnotes the result is bitwise identical across ranks); the 2-stage kernel\npartitions by size and rotates accumulation order per rank, so it cannot be\ninvariance-safe.\n\n**4. Drop unaudited backends** (`cuda_communicator.py`). Under batch\ninvariance, exclude AITER's custom all-reduce from the dispatch chain.\nFlashInfer is already excluded upstream by #52998, which landed the same guard\nwhile flipping the FlashInfer default on; this change builds on that guard\nrather than repeating it. QuickReduce is not excluded — it is constructed only\non ROCm (`current_platform.is_rocm()`), so it is unreachable on the CUDA\nconfigurations validated here, but on ROCm it would be tried first. Under batch\ninvariance on CUDA the chain is `['CUSTOM', 'PYNCCL']`.\n\nThe env pin in `override_envs_for_invariance()` is retained as\nbelt-and-braces: since #52998, the communicator itself excludes FlashInfer\nunder batch invariance, so the pin is redundant for dispatch but guards\nagainst future refactors of that logic.\n\n**5. Stop forcing the disable** (`config/parallel.py`), now that (1) makes the\ndecision fixed for the process lifetime. The platform-support disable\n(`if not current_platform.use_custom_allreduce()`) is untouched.\n\n**6. Test + docs.** A TP≥4 batch-invariance test in `tests/v1/determinism/`\n(the suite currently runs everything at TP=1, so the all-reduce path was\nuntested — that gap is why #50136 was possible to write), and\n`docs/features/batch_invariance.md` updated to describe the new behaviour.\n\n### Validation\n\nBitwise comparison of output token IDs **and** per-step logprobs as raw float32\nbit patterns (not `allclose`) across batch compositions: bs=1 reference, full\nmixed batch, shuffled batch, and tile-boundary batches of 17 and 65.\n\n| Arm | Config | Invariance | Custom AR engaged | Largest tensor |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| 1 | dense, TP=4, patched | clean | 4/4 ranks, 9918 calls each | 25,165,824 B |\n| 2 | dense, TP=4, `disable_custom_all_reduce` (control) | clean | none, as expected | — |\n| 3 | dense, long context (2048/4096) | clean | 4/4 ranks | 25,165,824 B |\n| 4 | MoE (Qwen3-30B-A3B), TP=4 | clean | 4/4 ranks | 33,554,432 B |\n| 5 | fault injection (buffer forced to 4 KiB) | raises | — | — |\n| 6 | throughput, patched vs control | clean both | 4/4 vs none | 25,165,824 B |\n\nThree things make those greens checkable rather than merely green.\n\n**The bound predicts the observed maximum exactly.** Dense: largest tensor\nthrough the custom kernel = 25,165,824 B = `8192 × 1536 × 2`. MoE:\n33,554,432 B = `8192 × 2048 × 2`. Both are exactly\n`max_num_batched_tokens × hidden_size × dtype_size`, which is the formula the\nbuffer is sized from. Resulting buffers were 30 MiB and 40 MiB per rank.\nEngagement counts are read from each worker with `collective_rpc`, so they are\nexact per-rank values (`calls=9918`, identical on ranks 0–3), not log-derived\nestimates.\n\n**Every patched arm crossed the old threshold.** 25,165,824 B is 3.0× the\n8 MiB constructor default that applied before this change, and the MoE arm's\n33,554,432 B is 4.0×. (The `CUSTOM_ALL_REDUCE_MAX_SIZES` clamp is unreachable\nunder batch invariance, since `override_envs_for_invariance()` sets\n`VLLM_ALLREDUCE_USE_SYMM_MEM=0`.) So these runs exercised tensors that\npreviously fell through to NCCL — they sat in the regime where the gate used to\nflip, not one where it never would have.\n\n**Both guards fire with their intended messages.** Injecting a 4 KiB buffer so a\nreal all-reduce exceeds it:\n\n```\nRuntimeError: custom all-reduce received a 25165824 byte tensor, above the 4096\nbyte batch-invariant buffer. Falling back to NCCL would break batch invariance.\nRe-run with VLLM_BATCH_INVARIANT unset, or file a bug with the model and\nmax_num_batched_tokens so the bound can be corrected.\n```\n\nAnd `VLLM_CUSTOM_ALLREDUCE_ALGO=2stage` under `VLLM_BATCH_INVARIANT=1`:\n\n```\nVLLM_CUSTOM_ALLREDUCE_ALGO=2stage is incompatible with VLLM_BATCH_INVARIANT=1:\ncross_device_reduce_2stage partitions the message by size and accumulates in a\nrank-rotated order, so an element's summation order changes with message size\n(i.e. with batch composition) and differs across owning ranks.\n```\n\nThroughput, batch-invariant mode, same workload, identical output (14,784\ntokens in both arms):\n\n```\npatched (custom AR)    8006.4 tok/s   TTFT p50 53.40 ms   TPOT p50  8.48 ms\ncontrol (NCCL only)    3906.0 It does three things:\n\n- adds an opt-in `symm_mem` weight-migration backend;\n- fuses EPLB's logical→physical replica mapping and expert-load recording into the DeepGEMM MegaMoE staging kernels;\n- takes async EPLB's host work off the decode critical path.\n\nNot a duplicate. No open or merged PR adds a symmetric-memory EPLB backend or fuses the MegaMoE mapping. #61176 and #61839 enable EPLB for the ROCm AITER MegaMoE backend. Related: #61767 (`cpu_all_reduce_`, reused here), #57549, #56716, #61643.\n\n## Claims\n\n- EPLB raises decode throughput on DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash on GB200 NVL72 by +3.7% at DEP32 with 72 req/rank and +7.7% at DEP32 with 128 req/rank. At DEP16 the step is about 3% shorter, but the throughput gain is within run-to-run noise.\n- `symm_mem`: senders push expert rows into the receiver's `expert_buffer`, which is allocated in CUDA symmetric memory. There is no staging buffer, and it needs no memory beyond `expert_buffer`. Cross-node peer copies run at about 800 GB/s, against about 88 GB/s for NIXL, and a 40-layer rearrangement transfers in 0.3–0.6 s at DEP32.\n- MegaMoE layers no longer launch a separate EPLB mapping kernel, which saves 5–6 µs per layer. The replica choice is shared with the router through one Triton helper, so physical ids and load counts match `eplb_map_to_physical_and_record` exactly.\n- Async EPLB no longer adds a GPU→host sync to the decode loop. It also raises on every rank when the worker fails on any rank, instead of stalling.\n\n## Validation\n\nHardware: GB200 NVL72, 4 GPUs per node.\n\nServing setup: DSpark with 5 draft tokens, MRV2, FULL CUDA graphs, async scheduling.\n\nEPLB setup: `{\"num_redundant_experts\": <DP>, \"window_size\": 1000, \"step_interval\": 3000, \"use_async\": true, \"communicator\": \"symm_mem\"}`, i.e. one redundant expert per rank.\n\nWorkload: decode only, coding-agent prompts with mean ISL ≈ 85k (prefix-cached), 16k output tokens with `ignore_eos`, and requests pinned evenly to DP ranks. Throughput is the output rate over the window in which every request is decoding.\n\nOff and on arms ran interleaved (ABAB) on the same nodes. These runs used a branch of nightly fe4df3df (2026-10-12) with this change set and #61767, before the rebase and without the second commit.\n\n| Config | EPLB off (tok/s) | EPLB on (tok/s) | Δ | Welch t |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| DEP16, 72 req/rank | 223.6k ± 2.0k (n=4) | 228.1k ± 4.2k (n=4) | +2.0% | 2.0 (not resolved) |\n| DEP32, 72 req/rank | 419.5k ± 1.3k (n=4) | 435.1k ± 5.8k (n=4) | +3.7% | 5.3 |\n| DEP32, 128 req/rank | 500.7k ± 5.1k (n=3) | 539.4k ± 9.7k (n=3) | +7.7% | 6.1 |\n\n- DEP16 step time, p50 of 2000-step windows at full batch, same build: 24.34 / 24.28 ms off vs 23.61 / 23.25 ms on.\n- At DEP32 with 128 req/rank, one pass per arm is excluded. A host stall unrelated to EPLB cut that pass by 13–34%.\n\nPer-step breakdown, DEP32 with 128 req/rank:\n\n| | EPLB off | EPLB on |\n|---|---|---|\n| Step time (CUDA events, unprofiled) | 38.8 ms | 36.5 ms |\n| GPU idle at the step boundary | 0.27 ms | 0.27 ms |\n| Main-model MegaMoE (5 s CUDA profile) | 18.1 ms | 13.4 ms |\n| All other kernels (same profile) | 21.5 ms | 21.2 ms |\n\nThe GPU never waits on the host, so the MegaMoE saving turns into step time. MegaMoE is about 40% of the step, which caps the end-to-end gain.\n\nMicrobenchmarks (GB200):\n\n- **Fused EPLB staging**, one MoE layer, hidden 5120, top-k 6, 384 logical experts. Within one node, `symm_mem` and NIXL (CUDA IPC) move weights at a similar rate; `symm_mem`'s advantage is across nodes over multi-node NVLink.\n\n## Details\n\n**`symm_mem` backend**\n- The receive buffer is one symmetric `uint8` buffer per EP group, set up with a single rendezvous and carved into per-tensor views. The backend checks the NVLink/MNNVL domain before allocating.\n- Senders and receivers derive each receive row from the global placement with the same helper, so `add_recv` is a no-op. Push backends get every rank's rows through a new `recv_rows` argument to `set_transfer_context`. This argument is optional and other backends ignore it.\n- Each `execute()` runs two CPU barriers. GPU spin barriers can deadlock against MegaMoE's cooperative launches.\n- Rejected with elastic EP. -20.88975022582038, "excerpt_truncated": true}, {"id": "arxiv:2609.32278", "source": "arxiv", "kind": "paper", "url": "https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32278v2", "title": "RR-Evict: Fine-Grained Prefix Cache Eviction beyond LRU for Agentic LLM Serving", "body": "LLM-based agents execute long-horizon tasks through repeated model calls interleaved with tool execution and user interaction. As each call extends the history accumulated in previous turns, prefix caching avoids repeated prefill of the agent's entire context. However, the aggregate cache footprint grows with context length and concurrency, forcing serving systems to reclaim cached KV tensors. We identify recency synchronization: accesses to an agent's cached history refresh its cache nodes together, causing least recently used (LRU) eviction to concentrate on a few agents' private histories. When a fully evicted agent returns, it must recompute nearly its entire accumulated context, producing a large time-to-first-token (TTFT) outlier even if most other requests retain substantial cache reuse. We present RR-EVICT, a fine-grained prefix-cache eviction strategy that distributes reclamation across idle agent trajectories. RR-EVICT visits agents in round-robin order and evicts a tail chunk from each, preserving reusable prefixes for more agents when capacity remains for idle state. Returning agents can reuse these partial histories and recompute smaller missing suffixes. The policy requires no prediction of future arrivals or tool latency. We implement RR-EVICT in SGLang and evaluate conversational and coding-agent workloads under colocated and prefill-decode-disaggregated serving. Compared with LRU, RR-EVICT reduces P99 TTFT by up to 75.4% and P99 uncached prompt tokens by up to 65.7%.", "created_at": "2026-09-26T06:09:15Z", "updated_at": "2026-10-02T21:09:45Z", "fetched_at": "2026-10-19T20:49:02Z", "state": null, "merged_at": null, "parent": null, "tags": ["ttft", "performance", "kv-cache"], "metadata": {"read_depth": "abstract", "authors": ["Zaifeng Pan", "Chris Wu", "Zhengding Hu", "Xinwei Qiang", "Zhongkai Yu", "Yufei Ding"], "query": "LLM serving prefix cache admission eviction reuse prediction memory pressure"}, "content_hash": "b4094ffac6c4a0830814e1133a2f4d4be45e6d438db20c1b0ed5948f30318e3a", "rank": -28.216039120496703, "excerpt_truncated": false}, {"id": "github:vllm-project/vllm:46938", "source": "github", "kind": "pr", "url": "https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/46938", "title": "[Misc] Report prefix cache hit rate in vllm bench serve", "body": "## Purpose\r\n\r\n`vllm bench serve` reports latency and throughput, but not the prefix cache\r\nhit rate — so benchmarking prefix caching means manually scraping `/metrics`\r\nand computing the rate by hand. That is error-prone: the Prometheus counters\r\nare cumulative since server start, and the server's logged hit rate uses a\r\nrolling 1000-request window — neither corresponds to \"this run\".\r\n\r\nThis mirrors the existing spec-decode / diffusion metric handling: snapshot the\r\n`vllm:prefix_cache_queries_total` / `vllm:prefix_cache_hits_total` counters\r\nbefore and after the main run and report the per-run hit rate (plus raw queries\r\nand hits) in the console summary and the saved JSON. bug\n\nOn hybrid Mamba2 models (e.g. NemotronH), `--enable-prefix-caching` is silently inert whenever `max_model_len` is smaller than the attention block size that vLLM auto-selects for the hybrid KV layout. Prefix caching is accepted without warning, but the cache never records a single hit, so users get none of the expected TTFT benefit and no indication why.\n\n**Mechanism.** For hybrid models, `Platform.check_and_update_config` (`vllm/platforms/interface.py`, the \"Setting attention block size to N tokens\" path) raises `cache_config.block_size` up to `attn_block_size` so the attention page size is >= the mamba page size:\n\n```python\nif cache_config.block_size < attn_block_size:\n    cache_config.block_size = attn_block_size\n    logger.info(\"Setting attention block size to %d tokens ...\", attn_block_size)\n```\n\nFor this model that yields `block_size = 8320`. Block-hash prefix caching only registers a **complete** block, so if `max_model_len < block_size` no request can ever fill one block, and the prefix cache is structurally unable to hit. Nothing warns the user that enabling prefix caching will do nothing in this configuration.\n\n### Reproduction\n\nSame model and GPU, only `--max-model-len` differs. Send the same prompt three times (temperature 0), measure streaming TTFT and read `/metrics`.\n\n**Inert — `--max-model-len 4096` (< block_size 8320):**\n\n```\n--enable-prefix-caching --max-model-len 4096   # block size auto-set to 8320\n```\n| request | TTFT |\n|---|---|\n| 1 (cold) | 0.349 s |\n| 2 (repeat) | 0.249 s |\n| 3 (repeat) | 0.259 s |\n\n```\nvllm:prefix_cache_queries_total  10608.0\nvllm:prefix_cache_hits_total     0.0      # zero hits across 10.6k queries\n```\n\n**Working — `--max-model-len 16384` (> block_size 8320), same prompt (~12.8k tokens):**\n\n| request | TTFT |\n|---|---|\n| 1 (cold) | 1.510 s |\n| 2 (repeat) | 0.313 s  (4.8x faster) |\n| 3 (repeat) | 0.315 s |\n\n```\nvllm:prefix_cache_queries_total  38448.0\nvllm:prefix_cache_hits_total     16640.0   # ~43% hit rate\n```\n\nSame model, same `block_size = 8320`; the only difference is `max_model_len`. Below the block size, hits are impossible; above it, prefix caching works as expected.\n\n### Impact\n\nAny hybrid-Mamba user (NemotronH, and likely other MambaMixer2 models whose page-size coupling inflates `block_size`) who runs with `max_model_len` below the auto-selected block size gets prefix caching that is enabled but completely inert, with no log message pointing at the cause. `max_model_len 4096` is a common setting, so this is easy to hit unknowingly.\n\n### Suggested fix\n\nAt config validation, when `enable_prefix_caching` is set and the resolved `cache_config.block_size > max_model_len` for a hybrid model, emit a warning that prefix caching will be ineffective in this configuration (block size exceeds max model len, so no full block can form), and suggest raising `--max-model-len` at or above the block size. "https://api.github.com/repos/vllm-project/vllm/labels/nvidia", "name": "nvidia", "color": "76B900", "default": false, "description": "", "archived_at": null, "archived_by": null}], "comments": 11, "state_reason": null}, "content_hash": "938add20a9dd4f06d4b77c1fd60a78dd8604bba028e0acea2af9070516b4a33d", "rank": -21.478844137224286, "excerpt_truncated": false}, {"id": "github:vllm-project/vllm:37530", "source": "github", "kind": "pr", "url": "https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/37530", "title": "[Bugfix] Fix MLA KV cache blocks not zeroed on reuse, causing CUDA crashes under concurrent load", "body": "<!-- markdownlint-disable -->\n\n## Purpose\n\nFix a CUDA illegal memory access crash in MLA (Multi-head Latent Attention) models under sustained concurrent load.\n\nReproduced with [kakaocorp/kanana-2-30b-a3b-instruct-2601](https://huggingface.co/kakaocorp/kanana-2-30b-a3b-instruct-2601) (DeepSeek V3 architecture) with FP8 dynamic quantization on NVIDIA H200.\n\nWhen KV cache blocks are freed by completed requests and reallocated to new ones, they retain stale data from the previous request. For standard attention (`FullAttentionSpec`), these blocks are zeroed before reuse via the `KVBlockZeroer` pipeline. However, MLA attention (`MLAAttentionSpec`) is completely excluded from this zeroing pipeline, so reused blocks contain stale KV data that corrupts FP8 computation and crashes CUTLASS kernels.\n\nThe crash occurs under **continuous concurrent load** because sustained traffic causes frequent block alloc/free cycles, leading to stale data accumulation in reused blocks. It is reproducible both with and without prefix caching, though disabling prefix caching (`--no-enable-prefix-caching`) makes it deterministic since blocks are directly reused without hash-based validation.\n\n**Root cause:** `MLAAttentionSpec` is a subclass of `FullAttentionSpec`, but three places use exact type matching (`type(x) is FullAttentionSpec`) instead of `isinstance()`, excluding MLA from the entire block zeroing pipeline:\n\n1. **`vllm/v1/kv_cache_interface.py`** — `needs_kv_cache_zeroing` only checks `has_mamba_layers`. Since this property gates the entire zeroing pipeline (kernel initialization, block ID collection, and GPU zeroing execution), MLA models never have any blocks zeroed.\n\n2. **`vllm/v1/core/single_type_kv_cache_manager.py`** (2 occurrences) — `type(self.kv_cache_spec) is FullAttentionSpec` in `allocate_new_blocks()` and the external-computed-tokens path excludes MLA blocks from the `new_block_ids` collection. Even if the pipeline is enabled, no MLA block IDs are ever collected for zeroing.\n\n3. **`vllm/v1/worker/utils.py`** — `type(spec) is not FullAttentionSpec` in `KVBlockZeroer.init_meta()` skips MLA layers when building the GPU memory address table for the Triton zeroing kernel. Without MLA segment addresses, the zeroing kernel cannot zero MLA KV cache memory.\n\nAll three must be fixed together — the pipeline has a gate (1), data collection (2), and execution (3) stage, and MLA was excluded from all of them.\n\n**Fix:**\n- Add `has_mla_layers` property and include it in `needs_kv_cache_zeroing`\n- Change `type(x) is FullAttentionSpec` → `isinstance(x, FullAttentionSpec)` (2 files, 3 occurrences)\n\n## Test Plan\n\n- **Serving benchmark** — Verify the model serves correctly under concurrent load with variable-length inputs:\n\n```bash\n# Start server\nvllm serve --random-range-ratio 0.3 \\\n  --ignore-eos \\\n  --seed 42\n```\n\n> **Note:** `--random-range-ratio 0.3` creates variable input/output lengths that diversify block alloc/free patterns, triggering Batch invariance means bitwise-identical results, which is what every other test in `tests/v1/determinism/test_batch_invariance.py` checks with `torch.equal`. With the tolerance the NVFP4 test can pass while the NVFP4 GEMM is in fact batch dependent.\n\nThis PR replaces the comparison with `torch.equal` and prints both logprob lists on failure. No product code changes.\n\n## Test Plan\n\n- `tests/v1/determinism/test_nvfp4_batch_invariant.py` with the default model (`nm-testing/TinyLlama-1.1B-Chat-v1.0-NVFP4`), before and after the change, with the default settings and with `VLLM_NVFP4_NEEDLE_TRIALS=5 VLLM_NVFP4_NEEDLE_BATCH_SIZE=32`, on one GB300 (SM103) GPU.\n- Supporting evidence that NVFP4 checkpoints are bitwise batch invariant on Blackwell with the current kernels: online bs=1-vs-batched logprob checks for `nvidia/Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct-FP4` and `nvidia/Qwen3-30B-A3B-FP4` at TP=1 and TP=4 on `main` (`d09ff77637`).\n\n## Test Result\n\nThe \"after\" runs were made on a GB300 checkout of `main` that also carries the local patches of the separate PRs #61035/#61036/#61037/#61038 (none of them touch this test or the NVFP4 kernels); the `COMMIT:` lines show the base commit and the number of locally changed files. This isolates the crash to the LoRA path.\n\n2. **The dtype mismatch was fixed but the crash persists**: NVFP4 kernels were outputting `float32` (from `torch.get_default_dtype()`), while LoRA expects `bfloat16`. This suggests a memory layout or alignment issue with how NVFP4-quantized layer outputs are passed to the LoRA Triton kernels — the tensor layout/strides from NVFP4 dequantization may not match what the Triton kernel expects.\n\n## Steps to Reproduce\n\n1. "url": "https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53630", "title": "[Spec Decode] Verify in head dtype and drop the FP32 chunk workaround", "body": "## Purpose\n\nSpeculative decoding expands each request into multiple verification positions. Whenever\nany active logits processor is present, the target rejection verifier currently\nmaterializes an FP32 processing canvas of shape `[num_verification_tokens, vocab_size]`.\nThe production path bounds the transient allocation with a 1 GiB chunk, which in turn\nforces a cap on the total logits adaptive verification may schedule per step.\n\nThis PR makes the target verifier explicitly opt into storing and processing logits in\nthe LM head dtype (BF16/FP16), and deletes the FP32 canvas, the chunk iterator, and the\nadaptive cap:\n\n- temperature is applied in FP32 when the rejection kernels, top-k/top-p, and the\n  processed-logprob scorer load the logits; it is never written back to the stored\n  logits;\n- top-k/top-p computes its boundary in FP32 and writes back only the mask; penalties are\n  computed in FP32 and written back to the head dtype with a single rounding; exact mask\n  processors keep their original semantics;\n- raw reporting keeps an original head-dtype snapshot only when a processor would\n  overwrite the input; processed reporting reads the processed stored logits;\n- the adaptive cost tables, selection formula, grammar mapping, and zero-budget contract\n  are unchanged; only the hard cap that existed to serve the FP32 chunk is removed;\n- the PyTorch top-p fallback's softmax and cumulative sum are pinned to FP32, since\n  head-dtype target logits make the previously latent BF16 accumulation path reachable;\n- speculative decoding fails closed on any nonzero `min_p`, and custom\n  `logprob_token_ids` reporting is wired up.\n\nThe regular sampler, non-speculative decoding, and zero-draft steps keep the existing\nFP32 processing path.\n\n<details>\n<summary>Supported sampling processors and reporting behavior</summary>\n\nAll supported processors below avoid materializing a full-vocabulary FP32 verifier canvas. This\nPR does not change which processors are supported by speculative decoding.\n\n| Sampling parameter / processor | Property | Supported in Spec Decode | Effect on stored logits | Head-dtype precision |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| temperature | Distribution scaling | Yes (unchanged) | None; consumers scale on load | Computed in FP32 |\n| top_k / top_p | Selection mask | Yes (unchanged) | Preserve kept values; mask others with `-inf` | Exact |\n| repetition / frequency / presence penalties | Value transformation | Yes (unchanged) | Rewrite affected values | One BF16/FP16 rounding |\n| allowed_token_ids / min_tokens / bad_words | Hard token mask | Yes (unchanged) | Write `-inf` masks | Exact |\n| thinking_token_budget | Forced-token override | Yes (unchanged) | Write a dtype-safe sentinel | Semantically exact |\n| structured-output grammar mask | Hard token mask applied before verification | Yes (unchanged) | Write `-inf` masks | Exact |\n| min_p | Probability filter | No (unchanged) | — | Any nonzero value fails validation |\n| logit_bias | Additive value transformation | No (unchanged) | — | One BF16/FP16 rounding |\n\nRaw and processed logprobs are reporting modes rather than logits processors. Raw reporting reads\na pre-processor head-dtype snapshot when stored logits may be modified. Processed reporting reads\nthe processed stored logits and applies temperature in FP32 on load. Adaptive-verification output\nlogprobs were enabled upstream by #52242 (merged 2026-08-25); this PR is rebased on top of it and\npreserves its device-tensor boundary reporting through the head-dtype scorer.\n\n</details>\n\nPenalties are the only path that writes back to the stored logits lossily: the processor\nstill computes in FP32, but the result is rounded to BF16/FP16 once. Please pay\nparticular attention to the penalty-only, stochastic, and `top_k=1` results under\n**Numerical fidelity and model quality** below. We did not find an implementation that\npreserves FP32-equivalent penalty output without either reintroducing full-vocabulary\nFP32 storage or adding significant extra scans/latency; this PR therefore keeps this\nnumerical trade-off explicit rather than claiming processed penalty logprobs are bitwise\nFP32.\n\n### Why this is not a repeat of #48641\n\n#48641 removed the same FP32 copy and was reverted in full three days later by #49033\nover a never-root-caused DP+EP+MTP hang. Beyond removing the copy, #48641 also changed\nthe numerics: it applied temperature by **writing the scaled values back into the\nstored head-dtype logits**, for the regular sampler and the speculative verifier\nalike. Every such write-back costs an extra BF16/FP16 rounding, and every downstream\nconsumer — rejection statistics, top-k/top-p, logprob reporting — then reads the\nalready-rounded, temperature-scaled values. Whether or not that contributed to the\nhang, it is a real precision hazard, and this PR is built specifically to remove it:\n\n- stored logits always keep their pre-temperature values; temperature is applied in\n  FP32 registers at every load site and never touches storage;\n- top-k/top-p computes its boundary in FP32 and writes back only `-inf` masks — kept\n  logits are bit-identical to what the LM head produced;\n- the only remaining lossy write-back is penalties, which is quantified and\n  guardrailed under **Numerical fidelity and model quality** below;\n- the scope is the speculative verifier only; the regular sampler keeps its FP32 path.\n\nOn the risk side, a permanent e2e test covers both #49033's pure-greedy control path\nand an active path that actually enters the head-dtype verifier, in the same\ndistributed engine. That test guards the risk quadrant; it does not claim to reproduce\nor explain the root cause of the original hang.\n\n### Related work\n\n- **#53090** (open) fuses only a temperature-only fast path; other processors or\n  processed logprobs still fall back to the FP32 canvas, so it cannot delete the\n  chunking or the adaptive cap. This PR covers every currently legal\n  processor/reporting combination.\n- **#50910** (merged) already adopted the \"store in head dtype, upcast to FP32 and\n  divide by temperature on load\" contract on the draft side; this PR aligns the target\n  verifier to the same contract.\n- **#47808**'s `max_total_logits` cap exists only to guarantee the FP32 verifier lands\n  in a single safe chunk; this PR removes that premise and does not change the adaptive\n  verification algorithm.\n- **#40704** is porting n-gram drafting to Model Runner V2; until it lands, n-gram\n  still falls back to Model Runner V1, so the custom `logprob_token_ids` e2e request\n  temporarily covers Eagle only.\n- **#52242** (merged 2026-08-25, while this PR was in flight) lifted the\n  adaptive-verification output-logprobs restriction. "[Kimi K3] Add EPLB support", "body": "## Summary\n\nAdd complete Kimi K3 EPLB metadata and redundant-expert support for both the main Kimi Linear model and its MTP model.\n\nThis change makes Kimi K3 expose the model-level MoE metadata expected by the v1 EPLB controller, constructs FusedMoE/MegaMoE with the physical expert layout, loads checkpoint weights into redundant physical expert slots, and keeps physical-expert metadata synchronized after a rearrangement.\n\nThe implementation is intentionally model-specific. It does not change the generic EPLB policy, communication implementation, or scheduling algorithm.\n\n## Why this change is needed\n\nBefore this PR, Kimi K3 instantiated its MoE layers without exposing the complete `MixtureOfExperts` contract used by EPLB:\n\n- the model did not report its MoE layer list to `EplbState`;\n- logical, physical, redundant, and rank-local expert counts were not tracked consistently;\n- FusedMoE was not constructed with Kimi's EPLB/redundant-expert configuration;\n- MegaMoE only allocated the original logical experts;\n- checkpoint expert mappings did not populate redundant physical slots;\n- the MTP MoE container did not register its expert layers with EPLB.\n\nAs a result, the generic EPLB controller could not safely build and update Kimi K3 expert maps.\n\n## Implementation\n\n### Main Kimi K3 model\n\n`vllm/models/kimi_k3/nvidia/model.py` now:\n\n1. Derives the expert layout from the runtime parallel configuration:\n\n   - `n_logical_experts` remains the checkpoint/model expert count;\n   - `n_redundant_experts` comes from `EPLBConfig` when EPLB is enabled;\n   - `n_physical_experts = n_logical_experts + n_redundant_experts`;\n   - `n_local_physical_experts = n_physical_experts / ep_size`.\n\n2. Validates physical-expert divisibility only for paths that require it: MegaMoE or EPLB. This avoids changing ordinary non-EPLB FusedMoE behavior.\n\n3. Constructs MegaMoE with the physical expert count and correct rank-local physical range.\n\n4. Constructs FusedMoE with:\n\n   ```python\n   enable_eplb=self.enable_eplb\n   num_redundant_experts=self.n_redundant_experts\n   ```\n\n5. Includes redundant physical slots in `fused_moe_make_expert_params_mapping`, so every redundant replica receives its initial logical-expert checkpoint weights.\n\n6. Adds `KimiK3MixtureOfExperts`, which exposes the model-level metadata required by EPLB:\n\n   - `moe_mlp_layers`;\n   - `moe_layers`;\n   - `num_moe_layers`;\n   - logical/physical/local/redundant expert counts;\n   - routed/shared expert counts;\n   - expert-group count.\n\n7. Implements `update_physical_experts_metadata()` so the model and individual Kimi MoE layers remain synchronized when the physical layout changes.\n\n8. Makes `KimiLinearForCausalLM` implement this shared Kimi K3 MoE interface and registers every real, non-PP-placeholder Kimi MoE layer.\n\n### MTP model\n\n`vllm/models/kimi_k3/nvidia/mtp.py` now:\n\n- makes `KimiK3MTP` implement `KimiK3MixtureOfExperts`;\n- registers all MTP MoE layers with the same metadata path as the main model;\n- includes redundant expert slots when constructing the MTP checkpoint expert mapping.\n\nThis keeps main-model and MTP expert registration consistent without duplicating EPLB bookkeeping logic.\n\n## Expert layout example used in validation\n\nThe full Kimi K3 checkpoint contains 896 logical routed experts.\n\nWith EPLB enabled in the validation run:\n\n```text\nlogical experts:    896\nredundant experts:   16\nphysical experts:   912\nEP size:              16\nphysical slots/rank:  57\n```\n\nThe control run retained the original identity layout:\n\n```text\nlogical experts:    896\nredundant experts:    0\nphysical experts:   896\nEP size:              16\nphysical slots/rank:  56\n```\n\n## End-to-end validation configuration\n\nTwo full GSM8K runs were executed. They used the same model, image, hardware topology, parallelism, model limits, weight-loading configuration, and request workload. The functional difference was whether EPLB rearrangement was enabled.\n\n| Setting | Value |\n| --- | --- |\n| Model | `moonshotai/Kimi-K3` |\n| Model revision |
