"github:vllm-project/vllm:43456", "source": "github", "kind": "issue", "url": "https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues/43456", "title": "[deepseek_v4] DeepSeekV4MTP loader silently skips top-level head.weight + embed.weight → 0% MTP draft acceptance with no error", "body": "## Summary\n\n`vllm.models.deepseek_v4.nvidia.mtp.DeepSeekV4MTP.load_weights` silently skips top-level `head.weight` and `embed.weight` when the saved artifact stores them at the top level (not as `mtp.0.head.weight` / `mtp.0.emb.tok_emb.weight`). The MTP layer's `shared_head.head` (ParallelLMHead) and `embed_tokens` (VocabParallelEmbedding) stay uninitialized → MTP draft head emits garbage logits → **0% MTP acceptance with no load-time error**. Speculative decoding produces draft tokens that are 100% rejected by the verifier.\n\n## Mechanism\n\n`DeepSeekV4MTP.load_weights` runs this loop:\n```python\nfor name, loaded_weight in weights:\n    name = name.replace(\"mtp.0.\", \"\")  # no-op on top-level keys like \"head.weight\"\n    spec_layer = get_spec_layer_idx(name)\n    if spec_layer is None:\n        continue  # ← top-level head.weight, embed.weight die here\n    ...\n```\n\nFor a key like `head.weight`:\n- `name.replace(\"mtp.0.\", \"\")` returns `head.weight` unchanged\n- `get_spec_layer_idx(\"head.weight\")` returns `None`\n- Loop hits `continue` → key never routed to the MTP layer\n- MTP layer constructed `shared_head.head` as ParallelLMHead but no weight ever assigned → keeps init random values\n\n## Repro\n\n1. Save a DSv4-Flash artifact via `transformers.save_pretrained` (the canonical path). MTP weights land at upstream naming `mtp.0.*` but `head.weight` + `embed.weight` stay at top level.\n2. Serve with `--speculative-config '{\"method\":\"mtp\",\"num_speculative_tokens\":1}'`.\n3. Watch `vllm:spec_decode_num_accepted_tokens_total / vllm:spec_decode_num_draft_tokens_total`. Result: drafts produced, accepted = 0.\n\nWe hit this exact scenario in [`canada-quant/dsv4-flash-w4a16-fp8-mtp`](https://github.com/canada-quant/dsv4-flash-w4a16-fp8-mtp) iteration 9. Smoke artifact had 797 `mtp.0.*` keys at first and got 0% acceptance silently. Diagnosis trace in [`FINDINGS_FOR_SIBLING.md`](https://github.com/canada-quant/dsv4-flash-w4a16-fp8-mtp/blob/main/FINDINGS_FOR_SIBLING.md) §C14.\n\n## Workaround (production-validated)\n\nPostprocess injects `mtp.0.head.weight` (FP32 copy of `head.weight`) and `mtp.0.emb.tok_emb.weight` (BF16 copy of `embed.weight`) as full duplicates. Working example: [`scripts/fixup_artifact.py`](https://github.com/canada-quant/dsv4-flash-w4a16-fp8-mtp/blob/main/scripts/fixup_artifact.py). The sibling artifact `canada-quant/DeepSeek-V4-Flash-NVFP4-FP8-MTP` applies the same pattern (799 mtp.* keys vs our pre-fix 797 — the 2 deltas are these aliases).\n\nAfter the workaround, MTP acceptance lands at 69.94% over 200 random prompts (raw data in [`benchmarks/phase2/acc_*.json`](https://github.com/canada-quant/dsv4-flash-w4a16-fp8-mtp/tree/main/benchmarks/phase2)).\n\n## Suggested fix\n\nOption A — `DeepSeekV4MTP.load_weights` should explicitly route top-level `head.weight` to `mtp.{N}.shared_head.head.weight` and top-level `embed.weight` to `mtp.{N}.embed_tokens.weight` (one line added before the `continue`):\n\n```python\nfor name, loaded_weight in weights:\n    # Re-route top-level head/embed to the MTP slot (model-level keys that\n    # the MTP draft tower needs but get filtered out by the spec-layer check)\n    extra = []\n    for layer_off in range(self.config.num_nextn_predict_layers):\n        if name == \"head.weight\":\n            extra.append((f\"mtp.{layer_off}.shared_head.head.weight\", loaded_weight))\n        elif name == \"embed.weight\":\n            extra.append((f\"mtp.{layer_off}.embed_tokens.weight\", loaded_weight))\n    if extra:\n        # process each routing manually + continue\n        for n, w in extra: weight_loader(n, w)\n        continue\n    # ... existing mtp.0. routing\n```\n\nOption B — raise at construction time when `shared_head.head` / `embed_tokens` end up uninitialized. Either is fine; silent 0% acceptance is the worst possible failure mode for spec-decode.\n\n## Severity\n\nAnyone who quantizes DSv4-Flash and ships an artifact via the standard `transformers.save_pretrained` path will hit this silently. The error message is misleading — it sounds like the kernel allows up to 2 speculative tokens, but it actually allows up to 2 total lookahead positions.\n\n## Reproducible on\n\n- vLLM `~/src/vllm` HEAD `50d9dd902` with cherry-picked PRs #43248+#43288+#43290+#43319\n- H200 SXM5 (Hopper, sm_90a), TP=2\n- DSv4-Flash W4A16+FP8+MTP artifact (`canada-quant/DeepSeek-V4-Flash-W4A16-FP8-MTP`, public)\n- `--attention-backend FLASHINFER_MLA_SPARSE`: same assertion fires (the paged_mqa_logits kernel is logits-side, not attention-backend-specific)\n\n## What I've ruled out\n\nDeepGemm has separate Blackwell-only assertions in `attention.hpp:210` and `:338` that constrain `arch_major == 10 and next_n == 1`. Those don't fire on Hopper. The `paged_mqa_logits.hpp:233` assertion is the active one on Hopper and accepts `next_n in {1, 2}`.\n\n## Impact\n\n`num_speculative_tokens=1` is the practical max on Hopper in this build. We measured 1.49× decode speedup at bs=1 with k=1 (TPOT median 6.02ms with MTP vs 8.93ms without). At k=2 the theoretical ceiling rises to ~1.85-2.0×, matching the sibling NVFP4-FP8-MTP B300 artifact's published 2.03× number. We're leaving ~25% of the MTP speedup on the table due to this assertion.\n\nRaw throughput data:\n- https://github.com/canada-quant/dsv4-flash-w4a16-fp8-mtp/tree/main/benchmarks/phase2\n- Summary: https://github.com/canada-quant/dsv4-flash-w4a16-fp8-mtp/blob/main/benchmarks/phase2/2026-05-22T195133Z-throughput-summary.md\n\n## Suggested fix\n\nTwo options:\n\n1. **Widen the assertion** in `smxx_fp8_fp4_paged_mqa_logits.hpp:233` to allow `next_n` up to whatever the kernel actually supports (presumably 4 or higher; DSv4-Flash's MTP block is designed for higher k). This requires DeepGemm-side changes in `deepseek-ai/DeepGEMM`.\n\n2. **Surface the constraint in vLLM's config validation** — when `--speculative-config num_speculative_tokens > 1` is set AND the model uses the paged_mqa_logits kernel path on Hopper, emit a clear error at startup rather than letting the cudagraph capture fail with a confusing assertion message.\n\nOption 2 is the cheaper path for vLLM-side. Option 1 unlocks the actual speedup.\n\n## Tracking\n\nFiled by the canada-quant team during W4A16+FP8+MTP quantization work. This is not intermittent — it is deterministic.\n\n### Root Cause\n\nThree layers of conflict:\n\n1. **NCCL on PCIe topology**: NCCL's default ring/tree communication pattern conflicts with b12x's barrier sync. During MTP verify, the all-reduce for draft token scoring and b12x's cross-GPU KV sync compete for the same PCIe bandwidth. Timing skew → deadlock.\n\n2. **b12x scheduling vs MTP dynamic batch**: b12x's CUDA Graph requires fixed input shapes and static `page_table_1` layout. MTP verify produces variable-length accepted tokens, causing `nsa_cache_seqlens` to diverge from graph-captured state at replay time.\n\n3. **NSA indexer dangling indices**: Draft tokens write temporary KV entries. If verify rejects them, KV cache rolls back but `topk_indices` from the indexer still reference the now-invalid entries → garbage attention output (e.g. XML tag leakage in tool calls).\n\n### Fix\n\n1. **NCCL patch**: Force NCCL to respect PCIe topology — route collective operations along the actual PCIe switch hierarchy instead of assuming uniform interconnect. This prevents barrier misalignment between MTP verify all-reduce and b12x KV sync.\n\n2. **Rewrite b12x scheduling logic**: Replace the static CUDA-Graph-only decode path with a topology-aware scheduler that dynamically selects execution path based on current phase (draft / verify / committed). The scheduler is aware of which GPU pairs share PCIe switches and which cross NUMA boundaries, and adjusts sync barriers accordingly.\n\n3. **Topology awareness**: On W790E-SAGE SE with 8 GPUs, PCIe switch topology and NUMA affinity determine TP communication latency distribution. Without topology-aware scheduling, NCCL picks worst-case paths → barriers never align → guaranteed hang.\n\n### Result\n\nAfter fix: TTFT < 0.3s, no hangs, MTP + b12x NSA coexist correctly.\n\n### Before submitting a new issue...\n\n- [x] Make sure you already searched for relevant issues, and asked the chatbot living at the bottom right corner of the [documentation page](https://docs.vllm.ai/en/latest/), which can answer lots of frequently asked questions.", "created_at": "2026-05-21T12:34:21Z", "updated_at": "2026-09-22T01:53:55Z", "fetched_at": "2026-10-19T20:53:07Z", "state": "closed", "merged_at": null, "parent": null, "tags": ["b200", "ttft", "performance", "speculation", "communication", "kv-cache"], "metadata": {"number": 43315, "labels": [{"id": 5135047172, "node_id": "LA_kwDOI7xefs8AAAABMhKaBA", "url": "https://api.github.com/repos/vllm-project/vllm/labels/bug", "name": "bug", "color": "d73a4a", "default": true, "description": "Something isn't working", "archived_at": null, "archived_by": null}, {"id": 7648877658, "node_id": "LA_kwDOI7xefs8AAAABx-icWg", "url": "https://api.github.com/repos/vllm-project/vllm/labels/stale", "name": "stale", "color": "ededed", "default": false, "description": "Over 90 days of inactivity", "archived_at": null, "archived_by": null}], "comments": 3, "state_reason": "not_planned"}, "content_hash": "4fb3513eec666e6fcd6baa6a0c9906eeafb49145281097ca70a0e7d26d9b4960", "rank": -13.232963484184399, "excerpt_truncated": false}, {"id": "github:vllm-project/vllm:60931", "source": "github", "kind": "issue", "url": "https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues/60931", "title": "WIP [RFC]: SupportsReload, a single post-load contract and reload verifier (follow-up to #59502)", "body": "\n\n## TL;DR\n\n#59502 makes reload cheap and safe for **quant methods**. The weakest part of reload is everything **around** them:\n\n- model-level `process_weights_after_loading` (PWAL) hooks that reload never calls;\n- `load_weights` code that writes params directly;\n- weight-derived tensors that nobody owns;\n- a global reload flag that every PWAL has to branch on.\n\nOn main, each of these silently serves stale or wrong weights.\n\nThis RFC proposes one small contract with a fixed place for every kind of post-load work. Model authors implement it, and the framework can **verify** it before an update starts:\n\n| Kind of work | Where it goes | Runs |\n|---|---|---|\n| Per-shard transform (TP narrowing, permutation, layout scatter, `-exp(A_log)`) | the param's `weight_loader` | on every load |\n| Per-module checkpoint → kernel conversion | module PWAL: **always transform, build once** | on every load, as the module completes |\n| Weight-derived values held by kernels (alphas, `1/a_scale`, bmm scales) | kernel `refresh()`, **called by the framework** | after every load, cold start included |\n| Cross-module derived values (mHC broadcast, DSpark stacks) | `SupportsReload.refresh_model()` | after every load, cold start included, last |\n| Non-weight setup (compile, device placement, geometry) | `post_load_setup()` | cold start only |\n\nFour consequences:\n\n1. **No global reload flag.** The `reload_mode()` / `is_reloading()` flag from #59502 goes away.\n2. **No model-level weight hook.** The free-form model `process_weights_after_loading()` stops doing weight work.\n3. **One sanctioned pattern** for model-specific changes to generic layers.\n4. **`SupportsReload` is a verifiable claim.** `verify_reload(model)` scans declarations, tensor ownership, the init snapshot vs the live model, load coverage, configuration, and post-update invariants. It returns SAFE, UNSAFE or UNVERIFIED per item, and the engine refuses unsafe updates under CUDA graphs.\n\n## Background: what main does today\n\nAll references are to main @ `240785b82c`.\n\n### Cold start\n`BaseModelLoader.load_model` (`model_loader/base_loader.py:62`) runs these steps in order:\n\n1. `initialize_model`, then `record_metadata_for_reloading` (the meta snapshot);\n2. `load_weights`;\n3. `process_weights_after_loading` (`model_loader/utils.py:133`), which itself runs:\n   - retie;\n   - quant-method PWAL for each module with a `QuantizeMethodBase`;\n   - attention PWAL (`dtype`);\n   - **model-level hook**.\n\nModules without a `quant_method` are visited by neither PWAL pass. The model hook exists mainly to reach them, through delegation loops like `for layer in layers: layer.ffn.finalize_mega_moe_weights()`.\n\n### Reload\nLayerwise reload (`model_loader/reload/layerwise.py`):\n\n1. restores checkpoint-format params on meta;\n2. buffers loader calls;\n3. replays them;\n4. runs quant PWAL;\n5. `copy_`s the results into the original tensors.\n\nIt does **not** call the model hook or retie.\n\n### Where post-load work lives today, and whether reload reproduces it\n\n| Location | Examples | On reload today |\n|---|---|---|\n| Constructor | conv1d `unsqueeze(1)`, alias buffers, padding fills | Safe: the snapshot is taken after `__init__` |\n| `weight_loader` | TP narrowing, Mamba `-exp(A_log)`, DSv4.1 permute-on-load (#60064), MegaMoE direct layout (#60096) | Safe |\n| Model `load_weights` code outside loaders | sink copies, DSv4 shared-expert padding, V3.2 FP8 indexer pairing, finalize-in-load | **Risky**: params are on meta, so direct writes are no-ops |\n| Quant PWAL | requant, transpose, repack, shuffle, **kernel build** | Values are right. Kernel objects and off-registry tensors are rebuilt at new addresses. |\n| Attention PWAL | KV scales, MLA absorption | Re-run. Host-float scales can't reach captured graphs. |\n| Model hook | MegaMoE, mHC, DSpark stacks, ROCm preshuffle, dots3 fusion, compile | **Not run**: stale or wrong |\n| Forward / lazy caches | GLM-5-Next `_merged_conv_weight` | **Stale** |\n\n### Problems this RFC addresses\n\n1. **Model hooks are cold-start only and have no reload contract.** There are 20 of them, all under `vllm/models/`; none are in `vllm/model_executor/models/`. They fall into these kinds:\n   - delegation;\n   - transform-then-drop: MegaMoE sets its raw params to `None`; dots3 runs `del self.experts`;\n   - derived-from-live;\n   - in-place rewrite: ROCm preshuffle, with no guard;\n   - storage aliasing;\n   - non-weight setup.\n2. **Direct writes skip the reload wrapper.** `param.data.copy_(...)` or `params_dict[n][:k].copy_(...)` in `load_weights` runs while params are on meta. `meta.copy_(cpu)` succeeds and does nothing (verified with PyTorch). The module never completes, and its old tensors are put back. Affected:\n   - gpt-oss sinks (`gpt_oss.py:723-728`);\n   - gpt-oss Quark experts;\n   - gpt-oss fused BF16 experts;\n   - DSv4 / V4.1 / HY-v4 `attn_sink`;\n   - mimo-v2 FP8 qkv;\n   - about 18 `default_weight_loader(param, …)` call sites.\n3. **PWAL mixes transforms with one-time work, and `reload_mode()` is how #59502 separates them.** Every PWAL author has to know about the flag. The one-time work includes:\n   - kernel construction: `Fp8MoEMethod._init_moe_kernel` runs on every PWAL (`fp8.py:693, 711`);\n   - quant-config capture;\n   - `register_buffer`;\n   - the AITER MLA precompile (~50 s).\n4. **Weight-derived state with no owner.** On reload these keep their old values while CUDA graphs keep reading their old addresses:\n   - plain tensor attributes: `hc_attn_fn_broadcast`, `_context_kv_norm_weights`, ROCm `_gateup_scale`;\n   - kernel-object tensors: `a1_gscale`, Triton MXFP4 `PrecisionConfig`;\n   - closures: CPU `layer.cpu_linear`.\n5. **Model-specific changes to generic layers are done ad hoc**, four different ways:\n   - a model-hook rewrite (ROCm preshuffle);\n   - a quant-method subclass (`DeepseekV4HeterogeneousMxfp4MoEMethod`);\n   - a quant-method swap (GLM-5.2 `GLM52LowLatencyLinearMethod`);\n   - an **instance monkeypatch** of `quant_method.process_weights_after_loading` (DSv4 CPU `wo_a`, `cpu/cpu_sparse.py:308`).\n6. **Nothing tells you in advance whether a reload will work.**\n   - The loader's \"all weights loaded\" check exempts every module whose `quant_method` defines PWAL (`default_loader.py:519-528`). That's every linear, embedding and MoE layer, so a missing `q_proj` loads silently.\n   - #59502's per-model `reload_safe` is an unchecked claim.\n\n## Proposed design\n\n### 1. Weight loaders: per-shard work, and \"eliminate\" first\n\n**Rule L1.** Every write to a loadable param goes through that param's `weight_loader`. `load_weights` only routes names. A model may wrap a param's loader (see §5) but must not write the param directly.\n\n**Rule L2.** If a transform is **separable per shard**, do it in the loader. Separable means each output byte depends on a single checkpoint tensor. Examples:\n- fixed row/column permutations (#60064 `permute_on_load`);\n- interleaves and scale repacks into kernel layout (#60096 MegaMoE: parameters created in DeepGEMM layout, `l", "created_at": "url": "https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51820", "title": "[BugFix] Clamp Mooncake store token_len to hash coverage under MTP/async scheduling", "body": "## Purpose\r\n- Under speculative decoding (MTP) combined with async scheduling, the scheduler can advance its token count optimistically before `block_hashes` are extended to match, tripping an assertion in `ChunkedTokenDatabase.process_tokens` and crashing the engine. \r\n\r\n- Clamp `token_len` to the range actually covered by `block_hashes` and save only that prefix instead of asserting; the remaining tail is picked up on a later step once the hashes catch up. Also log a warning when the clamp engages so the race stays observable.\r\n\r\n## Note on scope\r\nThis clamp stops the crash but does not fully close a smaller side effect: `KVCacheStoreSendingThread._record_saved` records the pre-clamp (optimistic) token count rather than what was actually saved, so the worker's own resume offset can advance past the clamped range and the skipped tokens for that block are never retried, leaving a small gap in what's persisted to the external Mooncake store for that request (generation correctness is unaffected). \r\nA more complete fix would have `_record_saved` (and the scheduler's `RequestTracker` bookkeeping) advance only by the actually-covered amount so the gap is retried instead of permanently dropped. That's left out of this PR since it touches block-lifecycle bookkeeping (`request_finished`/`delay_free_blocks`) and carries more risk than warranted for a crash hotfix; happy to follow up separately if the cache-hit-rate impact turns out to matter in practice.\r\n\r\n## Test plan\r\n\r\nFixes https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues/51805\r\n\r\n\r\n## Test Plan\r\nAdded unit tests for the adaptive DSpark verification for MiniMax M3 on ROCm. Part of the adaptive DSpark backend expansion (#51303): each model needs varlen-capable sparse kernels before adaptive verification can redistribute draft tokens across requests\r\nIt was validated end to end with amd/MiniMax-M3-MXFP4 and nvidia/MiniMax-M3-DSpark on MI325.\r\n\r\nScope: only the ROCm Triton indexer and sparse-decode paths gain ragged decode support. The CUDA and AITER MiniMax M3 backends still report device/CPU query-length mismatch as unsupported, so adaptive verification stays disabled there. With adaptive verification off, the uniform decode path is unchanged.\r\n\r\n## Changes\r\n\r\n- MiniMax M3 indexer and sparse attention (ROCm Triton): device-side ragged decode metadata; both AMD index-score launch policies, the fused top-k selector, and sparse decode map packed query tokens to requests from device `query_start_loc`.\r\n- `kernel_paged_attention_2d`: return for `query_len != 1` (previously `> 1`). FULL cudagraph replay can pad the request dimension with zero-length rows, which fell into the decode path and read one row past the logical query buffer.\r\n- Rejection sampler: adaptive verification allows the regular chunked path for batches with no draft tokens, whose logits layout isnever compacted.\r\n- Warmup: synthetic draft rows are bounded by the same rejection-sampler budget used at runtime, so large `max_num_seqs` configs nolonger fail at startup.\r\n- Adaptive verification overhead: confidence D2H copies use a non-blocking ring, and only TP rank 0 computes the draft budget, thenbroadcasts it.\r\n- DSpark config: prefer speculators-format top-level confidence-head fields and fall back to NVIDIA's legacy `dflash_config` fields.\r\n\r\n\r\nToken-to-request mapping uses upstream's `compute_token_to_req_indices`, a device-side binary search over `query_start_loc`. It doesn't depend on `max_query_len` and handles zero-length rows and the padding tail.\r\n\r\n## Not a duplicate\r\n\r\nSearched open PRs (adaptive verification, MiniMax M3 DSpark, token-to-request mapping, paged-decode zero-length). The closest is #52893, which skips AITER sparse PA under spec decode; this PR adds ragged decode to the Triton path instead.\r\n\r\n## Testing\r\n\r\nOn MI325:\r\n- `tests/v1/spec_decode/test_adaptive_verification.py`, `tests/v1/worker/test_gpu_rejection_sampler_chunking.py`, `tests/v1/worker/test_gpu_warmup_blocks.py`, `tests/v1/worker/test_attn_utils.py`: 138 passed, 1 skipped.\r\n- `tests/kernels/attention/test_minimax_m3.py`: all passed.\r\n- `test_prefix_prefill.py::test_contexted_kv_attention` (`chunked_prefill_paged_decode`, source build of this branch): 128 passed, 256 skipped.\r\n\r\n- Stress test of the ragged index top-k and sparse decode kernels at serving-sized batches: 60 random batches of 380–512 requests (1–8 query tokens each, up to 4K context, zero-length padding rows), reference-checked every 10th batch: all passed.\r\n\r\n## End-to-end results \r\n\r\nMI325, `nvidia/MiniMax-M3-DSpark` drafter with its default confidence head, `num_speculative_tokens=7`, FULL cudagraphs, default memory settings, `--max-model-len 4096 --max-num-seqs 512`. Fixed vs adaptive verification on the same build.\r\n\r\nGSM8K, full test set (1,319 questions, 5-shot, greedy, concurrency 64):\r\n\r\n| Checkpoint | TP | Fixed | Adaptive |\r\n|---|---:|---:|---:|\r\n| `amd/MiniMax-M3-MXFP4` | 2 | 0.8196 | 0.8226 |\r\n| `MiniMaxAI/MiniMax-M3-MXFP8` | 4 | 0.8779 | 0.8711 |\r\n| `MiniMaxAI/MiniMax-M3` (BF16) | 8 | 0.8704 | 0.8688 |\r\n\r\nRepeated MXFP8 runs vary by about ±0.01 for both modes (fixed 0.860–0.878 over 6 runs, adaptive 0.867–0.879 over 9 runs). It reuses the shared flattening selection introduced by [#52795](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52795) and keeps the ROCm capability scoped to the DeepSeek-V4 builder. NVIDIA and unrelated ROCm indexers are unchanged.\r\n\r\nThe ROCm sparse MLA/SWA declarations are similarly scoped. "github:vllm-project/vllm:60229", "source": "github", "kind": "pr", "url": "https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60229", "title": "[Spec Decode][GLM-5.3-Flash] Support adaptive verification", "body": "## Motivation\r\n\r\nAdaptive verification chooses on the GPU how many draft tokens to verify for each request. During full CUDA graph replay, the physical token buffer and captured control flow remain fixed, while the live request boundaries can change. GLM-5.3-Flash previously made two assumptions that were incompatible with this behavior:\r\n\r\n- GDN captured the ordinary one-token decode path, even though replay could group those token rows into fewer requests with multiple verification tokens.\r\n- The k-pool scatter path assumed every physical token row belonged to a live request, but a replayed graph can contain additional padding rows.\r\n\r\nThis PR updates both paths to handle GPU-selected request lengths and graph padding.\r\n\r\n## Changes\r\n\r\n- Allow the GDN backend to replay CUDA graphs whose per-request query lengths are chosen on the GPU.\r\n- Capture GDN’s speculative path and the k-pool indexer at the maximum configured verification width.\r\n- Update the k-pool scatter path to safely discard CUDA graph padding without synchronizing lengths back to the CPU.\r\n- Add tests for variable verification lengths, graph padding, and sparse-indexer alignment.\r\n\r\n## Testing\r\n\r\nAdded and ran focused tests covering:\r\n\r\n- Mixed per-request GDN verification lengths\r\n- CUDA graph padding in the k-pool scatter path\r\n- Sparse-indexer sequence-length alignment\r\n- Capture-time and replay-time request partition differences\r\n\r\n## Throughput results\r\n\r\nI benchmarked GLM-5.3-Flash with MTP on four B300 GPUs (TEP4). The sweep covered:\r\n\r\n- Speculative depths `kmax = 1...5`\r\n- 18 batch sizes from `B = 1` through `B = 256`\r\n- Prefix lengths `S = 512, 8192, 32768`\r\n\r\nThe table reports decode throughput for adaptive verification relative to fixed verification at the same maximum depth. Each value is the unweighted mean over the `(B, S)` at that speculative depth.\r\n\r\n| Maximum depth | Adaptive / fixed at same depth |\r\n|---:|---:|\r\n| 1 | 0.965× |\r\n| 2 | 0.958× |\r\n| 3 | 0.965× |\r\n| 4 | 1.065× |\r\n| 5 | 1.127× |\r\n\r\n---\r\n\r\n<details>\r\n<summary> Pull Request Checklist </summary>\r\n\r\n- [ ] I used vLLM's `/pr-checklist` skill. DSpark verification (#47808), which schedules ragged per-request draft budgets and requires device-sourced query lengths. Before this change K3's `FLASHINFER_MLA` reported `UNIFORM_BATCH`/`UNIFORM`, and the decode builders derived per-request query length from a measured/averaged count, so a graph captured on a uniform dummy did not match ragged replay.\r\n\r\n## Changes\r\n\r\n**FlashInfer MLA** (`flashinfer_mla.py`)\r\n- Promote `_cudagraph_support` to `AttentionCGSupport.ALWAYS` and `query_len_support` to `QueryLenSupport.VARLEN`.\r\n- On the causal spec-decode path, pass `cum_seq_lens_q` + `max_q_len` to `trtllm_batch_decode_with_kv_cache_mla` (flashinfer #3238); assert this path runs on trtllm-gen and does not return LSE (mutually exclusive with DCP).\r\n- Flatten non-causal and DCP decode rows by mapping each row to its request with `searchsorted` on the device query offsets instead of `repeat_interleave(uniform_len)`.\r\n- Carry `query_start_loc_device`, `cum_seq_lens_q`, and `max_query_len` on the decode metadata; set `cum_seq_lens_q` only when `max_query_len > 1`.\r\n\r\n**MLA common** (`mla_attention.py` + subclass builders)\r\n- Thread a promised `max_query_len` through `MLACommonMetadataBuilder._build_decode` (clamped to `reorder_batch_threshold`) and the `flashmla`, `flashattn_mla`, `rocm_aiter_mla`, and `dots3_note` builders.\r\n\r\n**KDA** (`kda_metadata.py`)\r\n- Promote `KimiK3KDAMetadataBuilder` to `AttentionCGSupport.ALWAYS`.\r\n- Route every active decode through the spec path by masking on `is_prefilling` rather than draft count.\r\n- Return `True` from `supports_device_cpu_query_lens_mismatch`.\r\n\r\n**KDA RecoverSSM** (`kda_metadata.py`, opt-in via `--use-replayssm`, off by default)\r\n- Make RecoverSSM co-exist with the adaptive path so it can drop the KDA spec-decode SSM state from `num_spec+1` slots to 1 per request (kernels already in #51855):\r\n  - Restore `self.layer_names` on the builder so RecoverSSM's commit context can resolve the per-layer forward context (the GDN base does not retain it; inert when RecoverSSM is off).\r\n  - Demote spec rows whose `query_len` exceeds `num_spec+1` out of the RecoverSSM path instead of raising. Adaptive's cost-table profiler probes token counts past the cudagraph limit by even-splitting them over the capped request slots, producing eager dummy rows longer than RecoverSSM's `num_spec+1` verify buffers; real captured/serving decode rows are always `<= num_spec+1`, so they are unaffected.\r\n\r\n**Mamba-hybrid model state** (`mamba_hybrid.py`)\r\n- Prefer the batch's promised `max_query_len`, and classify spec-decode rows off `is_prefilling` rather than `num_scheduled_tokens == draft_count + 1`.\r\n\r\n**K3 DSpark draft** (`dspark_mla.py`)\r\n- Build the confidence head when the config enables it, load its weights (skipped only when the draft did not build one), and expose `compute_confidence`.\r\n\r\nRelated Issue: https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues/51867\r\n\r\n## Test plan\r\n- [x] Updated the MLA backend metadata tests for the new `_build_decode`\r\n      signature (`test_mla_backends.py`, `test_rocm_aiter_mla_mtp_split.py`).\r\n- [x] Adaptive DSpark spec decode on Kimi-K3 (TP8, fp8 KV).\r\n- [x] RecoverSSM + adaptive on Kimi-K3: boots and serves through full-graph capture; validated against the base-adaptive checks (accuracy/coherence artifacts were overwritten by later runs and are being regenerated).\r\n\r\n## Validation\r\nKimi-K3 (target) + `Inferact/Kimi-K3-DSpark` (draft), TP8 on 8×B300, FP8 KV cache, `attention_backend=FLASHINFER_MLA`, `num_speculative_tokens=7`, `enable_adaptive_verification=true`. "github:vllm-project/vllm:44506", "source": "github", "kind": "issue", "url": "https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues/44506", "title": "[Feature][Spec-Decode]: Cascade: Utility-Driven Adaptive k for MoE Speculative Decoding in V1", "body": "### 🚀 The feature, motivation and pitch\n\n## Status Quo\nIn `vllm/v1/core/sched/scheduler.py`, `num_spec_tokens` is set once at engine init and never changes:\n\n```python\nself.num_spec_tokens = speculative_config.num_speculative_tokens\nif speculative_config.use_eagle():\n    self.use_eagle = True\n    self.num_lookahead_tokens = self.num_spec_tokens  # fixed for lifetime of engine\n```\n`num_lookahead_tokens` flows into `kv_cache_manager.allocate_slots()` which pre-allocates KV slots assuming every request in every batch will use exactly `k` draft tokens. Today, there exists no mechanism to vary `k` per-request or disable speculation for a specific request mid-flight without restarting the engine.\n\nThis is fine for dense models, however, **for MoE it is actively harmful**. On a dense model, verification overhead is roughly constant because the target model reads all weights regardless of how many draft tokens it verifies. On the other hand, on MoE, every additional draft token activates a different expert subset, so verification data movement scales with `k`, not batch size.  At typical math or reasoning acceptance rates, this makes speculation a net loss.  Cascade (https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.20675) quantifies this precisely across five MoE models.\n\nIn `vllm/v1/spec_decode/llm_base_proposer.py`, `propose()` has no per-request state. There is nowhere to track acceptance history, utility, or current `k` per request — the entire batch gets the same `num_spec_tokens`.\n\n## Proposed Solution\nUpstream [**Cascade** (MLSys 2026)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.20675) to vLLM V1. Cascade introduces per-request **speculation utility**:\n \n```\nutility = tokens_accepted / verification_cost(k)\n```\n\nTwo phases per request — short **test** phase probes k=1..K_max, long **set** phase locks the utility-maximizing K*. If utility < 1.0, speculation is disabled for that request without evicting the draft model.\n\nPaper results on H100: worst-case slowdown **54% → 5%**, throughput improvement **7–14%** over static k.\n\n## What changes in V1\nThree components need to change: \n- `per-request state` tracking in the proposer,\n- `per-request slot allocation` in the scheduler, and \n- `variable-length draft` handling in the model runner\n\nFor CUDA graphs, draft inputs will be padded to `K_max` with masked unused positions, consistent with how existing spec-decode graph captures handle variable batch sizes in `gpu_model_runner.py`.\n\n#### Validation target: NVIDIA B200 (Blackwell)\n\ncc @benchislett \n\n### Alternatives\n\nStatic k tuning reduces but does not eliminate the problem, optimal k varies per request and per task. Prior work #26504 explored `acceptance-rate-based dynamic k` but that does not account for expert activation overhead, which is the dominant cost on MoE verification. \n\n### Additional context\n\n- Cascade paper: https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.20675\n- MLSys 2026 poster: https://mlsys.org/virtual/2026/poster/10189\n- Prior variable-k PR: #26504\n- Spec-decode tracking issue: #28947\n- Q2 2026 roadmap: #39749\n\n### Before submitting a new issue...\n\n- [x] Make sure you already searched for relevant issues, and asked the chatbot living at the bottom right corner of the [documentation page](https://docs.vllm.ai/en/latest/), which can answer lots of frequently asked questions.", "created_at": "2026-06-04T07:20:32Z", "updated_at": "2026-10-06T01:54:24Z", "fetched_at": Dynamo then specializes on a full batch (duck-sized to `confidence_probs`), a single request, and a budget of 1.\r\n- `warmup()` runs batch sizes {1, max−1, max} × budgets {1, 2}. "github:vllm-project/vllm:57263", "source": "github", "kind": "pr", "url": "https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57263", "title": "[Spec Decode] Enable Gemma4 DSpark adaptive verification with FlashInfer", "body": "## Purpose\n\nEnable Gemma4 DSpark adaptive verification with FlashInfer TRTLLM-GEN, building on #52157 (#51871): graph-safe variable-length decode, trained confidence-head loading, graph-cache separation, and RoPE config normalization.\n\nAdaptive verification replays FULL decode CUDA graphs in which each request has 1 to 1 + k query tokens, read from the device `query_start_loc` (k = `num_speculative_tokens`). On `main` it requires every target builder to report `AttentionCGSupport.ALWAYS`, which also promises graphs for mixed prefill/decode batches. FlashInfer's TRTLLM-GEN path can replay variable-length decode batches but not mixed ones, so, per review, this PR declares that capability separately instead of adding an enum member:\n\n- **`AttentionCGSupport` is unchanged.**\n- **New builder classmethod** `get_varlen_cudagraph_max_query_len(vllm_config, kv_cache_spec) -> int | None`: the largest per-request query length L for which one FULL cudagraph replays any decode batch with 1 to L query tokens per request. It returns `None` for `ALWAYS` builders, which replay any batch, and for builders without variable-length support.\n\n| Builder | `get_cudagraph_support()` | `get_varlen_cudagraph_max_query_len()` | Adaptive verification |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `ALWAYS` builders (FA3, Triton, FlexAttention, Kimi-K3 KDA, FlashInfer MLA, ...) | `ALWAYS` | `None` | supported |\n| FlashInfer with its TRTLLM-GEN varlen path active (adaptive verification on, SM100 family, no DCP) | `UNIFORM_BATCH` | `1 + k` | supported (needs at least `1 + k`) |\n| FlashInfer otherwise, and other builders below `ALWAYS` | unchanged | `None` | not supported |\n\n- **Readers.** Adaptive verification is the only reader: every target builder must report `ALWAYS` or a bound of at least `1 + k`. CUDA graph mode resolution and capture are unchanged.\n- **Batches with a prefill no longer replay varlen decode graphs.** Dispatch matched those graphs on `max(num_scheduled_tokens)` alone, so decodes plus a prefill chunk of at most `1 + k` tokens replayed a decode graph. That is harmless for FlashInfer and FlashInfer MLA, which run short prefills on their decode kernels, but not for Kimi-K3's KDA builder (`ALWAYS` since #52988): it classifies rows by request state and does not restage its graph buffers when a prefill row is present. The runner now passes `max_query_len=None` for any batch with a prefill.\n- **Decode width on model runner V2.** Six copies of `1 + (2 if parallel_drafting else 1) * k` now share `max_decode_query_len()`. The 2× covers model runner V1's parallel drafter only, so on V2 the spec-as-decode width is `1 + k`, and DFlash/DSpark prefill chunks of `k + 2` to `2k + 1` tokens take the prefill kernel.\n\nNot a duplicate: #53929 proposes a `VARLEN_DECODE` member to enable adaptive DSpark verification for Qwen GDN; "url": "https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57632", "title": "[DFlash] Capture the context K/V precompute in the draft CUDA graph", "body": "## Purpose\r\n\r\nEven when the query forward replays a FULL CUDA graph, the DFlash/DSpark draft step still runs the context K/V precompute (`precompute_and_store_context_kv`) eagerly outside the graph, because the number of context tokens varies from step to step.\r\n\r\nThis PR captures the context K/V precompute ahead of the query forward in the draft's FULL decode graph. Each graph stores one full verify of context rows per request; rows past the batch are padded with `PAD_SLOT_ID` so they write no K/V, and any prefill context beyond the graph's rows is stored eagerly before replay. The precompute has no host syncs, so every DFlash drafter captures it. The draft attention metadata build stays eager, as before.\r\n\r\n\r\n## Validation\r\n\r\nCapturing the context K/V precompute must not change the speculator's proposals. The checks below therefore compare acceptance and accuracy between this branch's parent commit (`c723a831a8`) and `d8de1d3a2f`, and serving performance against current main, with everything else held fixed.\r\n\r\n### Setup\r\n\r\n- **Hardware / software:** 4× GB200, FlashInfer 0.6.18.post1, `VLLM_USE_V2_MODEL_RUNNER=1`.\r\n- **Model:** DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash (`dba1be0a40aa45a94ad051997016db3960a90277`).\r\n- **Speculative decoding:** DSpark with 5 speculative tokens and full decode CUDA graphs.\r\n- **Topologies:**\r\n  - **DEP4:** MegaMoE backend with adaptive verification.\r\n  - **TP4:** auto MoE backend with fixed verification.\r\n\r\n### Acceptance and GSM8K accuracy\r\n\r\nEach pair runs the full GSM8K test set (1,319 identical 5-shot completion prompts) with greedy decoding, concurrency 32, a 1,024-token output cap, and strict answer extraction.\r\n\r\nAcceptance length is read from the engine's `vllm:spec_decode_*` counters over the whole run for DEP4. For TP4, it is aggregated from the engine's periodic SpecDecoding log windows over the steady state; the same method is used on both sides of each pair.\r\n\r\n| Configuration | Acceptance length, incl. bonus (parent → PR) | GSM8K (parent → PR) |\r\n| --- | ---: | ---: |\r\n| DEP4 | 3.411 → **3.434** | 1234 → 1228 / 1319 (93.56% → 93.10%) |\r\n| TP4 | 3.471 → **3.463** | 1227 → 1219 / 1319 (93.03% → 92.42%) |\r\n\r\n- The acceptance deltas are within ±1% and have opposite signs across the two topologies, and the per-position acceptance curves coincide. support", "body": "seealso: #42845 https://github.com/deepseek-ai/DeepGEMM/issues/338\r\n## Purpose\r\n\r\nMegaMoE currently packs activations as FP8 (E4M3) in the symmetric buffer before dispatch. On Blackwell (SM100), FP4 (E2M1) packing would halve the buffer footprint and unlock the MXF4 mainloop (K=64 dense vs K=32 padded) in DeepGEMM.\r\n\r\nHowever, upstream DeepGEMM does not yet support FP4 activations end-to-end. The `fp8_fp4_mega_moe` kernel hardcodes `a_dtype_t = float_e4m3_t`, `get_symm_buffer_size_for_mega_moe` allocates input token buffers at FP8 width (`hidden` bytes/token), and the dispatch stage TMA loads assume FP8 layout. The `use_fp8_dispatch` parameter exists in the API signature but is not wired through to the buffer sizing or kernel logic. An RFC has been filed at deepseek-ai/DeepGEMM to track this.\r\n\r\nThis PR provides a **proof-of-concept** using `sgl-deep-gemm>=0.1.0`, which ships a `mega_moe_pre_dispatch()` CUDA kernel that handles E2M1 packing + FP4-aware buffer allocation. Two opt-in env vars, both default off:\r\n- `VLLM_DEEPGEMM_MEGA_MOE_USE_FP4_ACTS` -- use `deep_gemm.mega_moe_pre_dispatch()` for E2M1 activation packing instead of the Triton staging kernel\r\n- `VLLM_DEEPGEMM_MEGA_MOE_USE_MXF4_KIND` -- switches to `kind::mxf4` mainloop, requires FP4 acts\r\n\r\nWhen enabled, imports external `deep_gemm` for buffer allocation and pre-dispatch. When disabled, vendored `vllm.third_party.deep_gemm` is used as before -- zero change to default path.\r\n\r\nFlags forwarded to the external deep_gemm via `os.environ.setdefault(\"DG_USE_FP4_ACTS\", ...)` so explicit `DG_USE_*` overrides still win.\r\n\r\nFiles: `vllm/envs.py`, `vllm/model_executor/models/deepseek_v4.py`\r\n\r\n## Upstream Gap Analysis\r\n\r\nChecked deepseek-ai/DeepGEMM (latest, identical to vendored commit 891d57b for the mega module):\r\n\r\n- `csrc/apis/mega.hpp`: `get_symm_buffer_size_for_mega_moe` accepts `use_fp8_dispatch` but ignores it -- `input_token_buffer` is always `layout::Data(hidden)` (FP8, 1 byte/element)\r\n- `sm100_fp8_fp4_mega_moe.cuh` line 162-163: `a_dtype_t = cutlass::float_e4m3_t` -- UMMA configured for FP8 activations only\r\n- Dispatch pull stage: TMA loads `kHidden` bytes per token (FP8 width)\r\n- `slice_input_buffers` returns `x` as `[tokens, hidden]` float8, `x_sf` as `[tokens, hidden/128]`\r\n\r\nNone of these support FP4 activations. Full FP4 activation support requires changes at the C++/CUDA kernel level (buffer sizing, TMA load widths, UMMA A-matrix dtype), which is being tracked via the upstream RFC.\r\n\r\n## Test Plan\r\n\r\nHardware: 8x B200 (SM100, 183GB). Accuracy: GSM8K full test set (1319 samples), greedy decoding, max_tokens=2048, concurrency=32.\r\n\r\nDep: `pip install sgl-deep-gemm==0.1.0`\r\n\r\nV4-Flash (256 experts, hidden=4096, 43 layers), DP=4:\r\n\r\n```bash\r\n# w4a8 baseline\r\nvllm serve /path/to/DeepSeek-V4-Flash \\\r\n  --trust-remote-code --kv-cache-dtype fp8 --block-size 256 \\\r\n  --enable-expert-parallel --data-parallel-size 4 \\\r\n  --moe-backend deep_gemm_mega_moe --enforce-eager\r\n\r\n# w4a4 (this PR)\r\nVLLM_DEEPGEMM_MEGA_MOE_USE_FP4_ACTS=1 VLLM_DEEPGEMM_MEGA_MOE_USE_MXF4_KIND=1 \\\r\nvllm serve /path/to/DeepSeek-V4-Flash \\\r\n  --trust-remote-code --kv-cache-dtype fp8 --block-size 256 \\\r\n  --enable-expert-parallel --data-parallel-size 4 \\\r\n  --moe-backend deep_gemm_mega_moe --enforce-eager\r\n```\r\n\r\nV4-Pro (384 experts, hidden=7168, 61 layers), TP=8:\r\n\r\n```bash\r\n# w4a8 baseline\r\nvllm serve /path/to/DeepSeek-V4-Pro \\\r\n  --trust-remote-code --kv-cache-dtype fp8 --block-size 256 \\\r\n  --enable-expert-parallel --tensor-parallel-size 8 \\\r\n  --moe-backend deep_gemm_mega_moe --enforce-eager\r\n\r\n# w4a4 (this PR)\r\nVLLM_DEEPGEMM_MEGA_MOE_USE_FP4_ACTS=1 VLLM_DEEPGEMM_MEGA_MOE_USE_MXF4_KIND=1 \\\r\nvllm serve /path/to/DeepSeek-V4-Pro \\\r\n  --trust-remote-code --kv-cache-dtype fp8 --block-size 256 \\\r\n  --enable-expert-parallel --tensor-parallel-size 8 \\\r\n  --moe-backend deep_gemm_mega_moe --enforce-eager\r\n```\r\n\r\nEval: hit `/v1/chat/completions`, extract final number after `####`, compare against reference.\r\n\r\n## Test Result\r\n\r\n### Accuracy (GSM8K, 1319 samples, greedy)\r\n\r\nV4-Flash: w4a8 93.86% (1238/1319) -> w4a4 94.39% (1245/1319), delta +0.53%\r\nV4-Pro: w4a8 91.81% (1211/1319) -> w4a4 91.13% (1202/1319), delta -0.68%\r\n\r\nBoth within noise. No meaningful accuracy degradation from FP4 activation packing.\r\n\r\n### Performance (V4-Flash, DP=4, 8×B200)\r\n\r\nBenchmark config: 500 prompts, input_len=1024, output_len=8, max-concurrency=64, request-rate=inf, `--max-cudagraph-capture-size 512`, random dataset.\r\n\r\n| Metric | w4a8 | w4a4 | Delta |\r\n|---|---|---|---|\r\n| Request throughput (req/s) | 11.21 | 17.73 | **+58.2%** |\r\n| Total token throughput (tok/s) | 11,565.65 | 18,294.09 | **+58.2%** |\r\n| Output token throughput (tok/s) | 89.66 | 141.81 | **+58.2%** |\r\n| Mean TTFT (ms) | 3,789.39 | 1,834.52 | **-51.6%** |\r\n| Median TTFT (ms) | 832.51 | 639.82 | **-23.1%** |\r\n| P99 TTFT (ms) | 12,302.93 | 12,063.23 | -1.9% |\r\n| Mean TPOT (ms) | 272.46 | 251.78 | **-7.6%** |\r\n| Median TPOT (ms) | 75.91 | 69.89 | **-7.9%** |\r\n\r\nKey takeaway: ~58% throughput improvement, ~52% mean TTFT reduction. The gain is dominated by prefill (MXF4 mainloop K=64 dense vs K=32 padded). Decode TPOT improves ~8% from reduced buffer bandwidth.\r\n\r\n## What Happens When Upstream Supports FP4 Acts\r\n\r\nOnce deepseek-ai/DeepGEMM wires `use_fp8_dispatch=False` through to buffer sizing + kernel:\r\n1. Bump vendored commit in `cmake/external_projects/deepgemm.cmake`\r\n2. Replace `sgl-deep-gemm` path with a Triton FP4 staging kernel (~40 lines, E2M1 bucketize + pack) or use whatever staging API upstream provides\r\n3. Change `get_symm_buffer_for_mega_moe(..., use_fp8_dispatch=False)` -- the parameter already exists\r\n4. Remove import branches (3 sites), drop `sgl-deep-gemm` dep\r\n5. `DG_USE_FP4_ACTS` / `DG_USE_MXF4_KIND` env forwarding likely becomes unnecessary -- remove `_apply_mega_moe_dg_env`\r\n6. Move env vars from experimental to stable\r\n\r\n---\r\n<details>\r\n<summary> Essential Elements of an Effective PR Description Checklist </summary>\r\n\r\n- [ ] The purpose of the PR, such as \"Fix   # sm_90,   NEW\n```\n\n`DeepseekV4HopperMegaMoEExperts.finalize_weights` (one-shot weight\ntransform; idempotent):\n\n```python\nimport deep_gemm\n# DeepGEMM PR #323: SM90 weight transform = gate/up Mega-Gate fuses the gate GEMM and expert selection. The updated DeepGEMM pin allows non-finite padding scores, eliminating the separate hidden-state padding clear; existing input staging discards padded routes.\r\n\r\n**Mega-Gate achieves 1.18–1.31× routing speedup for M ≥ 32, with no meaningful speedup for M ≤ 16.** Speedup is baseline latency divided by Mega-Gate latency.\r\n\r\n| Tokens M | GateLinear + top-k (μs) | Mega-Gate (μs) | Speedup |\r\n| ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |\r\n| 1 | 6.640 | 8.096 | 0.820× |\r\n| 2 | 6.848 | 8.032 | 0.853× |\r\n| 4 | 6.752 | 8.032 | 0.841× |\r\n| 8 | 8.064 | 8.224 | 0.981× |\r\n| 16 | 8.256 | 8.256 | 1.000× |\r\n| 32 | 10.112 | 8.304 | 1.218× |\r\n| 64 | 10.353 | 8.448 | 1.225× |\r\n| 128 | 11.200 | 8.896 | 1.259× |\r\n| 256 | 11.488 | 9.696 | 1.185× |\r\n| 512 | 13.520 | 10.720 | 1.261× |\r\n| 1024 | 16.000 | 12.448 | 1.285× |\r\n| 2048 | 18.913 | 15.872 | 1.192× |\r\n| 4096 | 27.729 | 22.944 | 1.209× |\r\n| 8192 | 46.752 | 35.745 | 1.308× |\r\n\r\n**Method:** FlashInfer CUPTI timing of CUDA-graph replays with cold L2, 25 warmups, three rounds with alternating implementation order, and 100 samples per round. Values are medians of the round medians. Allocation, compilation, graph capture, input generation, routing metadata preparation, expert input staging, expert execution, and communication are outside the timed region. These measurements do not establish an E2E serving speedup.\r\n\r\n**Correctness:** Expert IDs matched exactly at every batch size; routing weights matched with `rtol=1e-3, atol=1e-4`. Synthetic inputs and separated correction biases avoid ambiguous top-k boundaries. An additional 48 CUDA-graph replay cases passed with NaN/Inf padding across text, image, and hash/image routing.\r\n\r\n**Versions:** vLLM `ef50fde183`, DeepGEMM `40d109bf`, PyTorch `2.13.0+cu130`, CUDA toolkit `13.1`, FlashInfer `0.6.18`, and CUPTI Python `13.0.1`.\r\n\r\n## Related work and AI assistance\r\n\r\nThis updates the existing Mega-Gate PR rather than opening another implementation. "2df585f06aa03927ce64896ac3708de726d5dc348a613cf59188ab4dfaa1ac0a", "rank": -18.773423090829915, "excerpt_truncated": false}, {"id": "github:vllm-project/vllm:61839", "source": "github", "kind": "pr", "url": "https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61839", "title": "[ROCm][DSv4] EPLB for the AITER MegaMoE backend", "body": "\n\n## Overview\n\nDeepSeek-V4's AITER MegaMoE backend (`--moe-backend aiter_mega_moe`) rejected `--enable-eplb`. This PR hooks it into vLLM's EPLB, the same way the DeepGEMM MegaMoE path does. On DeepSeek-V4-Pro, ATOM's EPLB for the same kernels cuts the long-context prefill step by 14.6%. The last three commits are generic EPLB changes that MegaMoE EPLB needs on ROCm; each can be split out.\n\n## Claims\n\n- `--enable-eplb` works with `--moe-backend aiter_mega_moe`. It rebalances by permutation only (`num_redundant_experts=0`), as ATOM does.\n- `EPLBConfig.max_rebalances` (new; default 0 = no limit, unchanged behaviour): stop after N rebalance points and keep the placement.\n- ROCm: `torch_nccl` expert-weight transfers no longer hang RCCL.\n- ROCm: the \"skip rearrange\" check no longer vetoes every MegaMoE rebalance.\n\nUsage (sync EPLB; `torch_nccl` requires `use_async: false`):\n\n```bash\nvllm serve deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4-Pro --data-parallel-size 8 --enable-expert-parallel \\\n  --moe-backend aiter_mega_moe --all2all-backend mori_high_throughput \\\n  --enable-eplb --eplb-config '{\"use_async\": false, \"communicator\": \"torch_nccl\", \"max_rebalances\": 2}'\n```\n\n## Validation\n\nThe numbers below come from a lab branch: vLLM 77154ca6 plus this change, alongside unrelated local patches. Setup: DeepSeek-V4-Pro on 8x MI355X, TP1 x DP8 attention, EP8 AITER MegaMoE, DSpark draft. EPLB ran with window 100 / interval 200 and at most 2 rebalances. The lab counted only engine steps that contain a prefill; that counting is not part of this PR, and a larger window and interval in engine steps give a similar cadence.\n\nPrefill probe: each of the 8 ranks prefills 8,107 new tokens at ~108K context next to 16 decoding requests:\n\n| | EPLB off | EPLB on |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| prefill step (ms, median of 6 rounds) | 1102 | 944 (-14.3%) |\n| MegaMoE combine wait per step (ms, mean over ranks; worst rank) | 167 (180) | 36 (55) |\n| MegaMoE stage1 / stage2 per step (ms) | 165 / 231 | 164 / 225 |\n| decode step at 16 req/rank (ms) | 55.6 | 55.5 - 56.3 |\n\nATOM gets -14.6% on the same probe. The gain is the combine wait: the expert GEMM time per rank stays the same, but the busiest rank no longer holds every rank back.\n\n- gsm8k (lm-eval, real speculative decoding) with EPLB on: 0.9719 / 0.9727 (two runs).\n- A rebalance moves ~2.5k of each rank's 2.9k expert slots across 61 layers. The first one took 5.2-5.7 s in the probe and load runs, ~2.3 s of it RCCL connection setup, and up to ~12 s under heavier live traffic. Later ones take ~2.5 s. ATOM's migration takes 5.4 s.\n- Stress test under load: 34 forced full-permutation rebalances while serving 32 concurrent requests, 0 request errors, correct greedy answers before and after.\n- Unit tests (CPU): `tests/distributed/test_eplb_utils.py` (ring pairing, cap, per-layer imbalance) and `tests/models/test_deepseek_v4_aiter_mega_moe.py -m cpu_test` (weight views, remap before the runtime choice, MixtureOfExperts registration; importing it needs a ROCm build).\n\nE2e and validation on this upstream branch are still to be added.\n\n## Details\n\n**MegaMoE EPLB (commit 1).**\n- The AMD `DeepseekV4ForCausalLM` implements `MixtureOfExperts` over the MegaMoE layers only; other AMD MoE paths keep `num_moe_layers == 0`.\n- `DeepseekV4AiterMegaMoEExperts.get_expert_weights()` returns per-expert row views of the finalized w13 / w2 weights and scales. The a16w4 shuffles are within-expert, so EPLB rewrites rows in place. MegaMoEV2 reads the weights by pointer at every launch, so CUDA graphs stay valid.\n- `forward()` maps logical to physical ids with `eplb_map_to_physical_and_record` (load recording included) before the runtime choice. The fixed-slot decode runtime (#61262) and the prefill runtime therefore both dispatch physical ids. Per-expert slot capacity does not depend on placement.\n- Redundant experts are rejected.\n- The DSpark draft is not registered: its MoE only sees decode-sized batches, and it keeps its own static placement.\n\n**`max_rebalances` (commit 2).** Every rebalance point blocks all EP ranks. A skipped point still pays an all-reduce, a host sync and the policy, and an applied one moves weights for seconds. When the placement only needs to adapt once (ATOM uses `ATOM_EPLB_MAX_REBALANCES=2` here), the cap stops further points; the remap stays and load recording stops.\n\n**ROCm ring P2P order (commit 3), root cause.**\n- `rearrange_expert_weights_inplace` posts a layer's sends and receives as one `batch_isend_irecv`: about 340 ops per rank here.\n- With RCCL 2.27.7 on MI355X this deadlocks on some transfer plans. The trigger is a rank that sends to many peers while it also exchanges with one peer in both directions.\n- It is deterministic for a given plan. A torch-only 8-GPU reproducer hangs on the same seeded plans every time, with one batch per layer and also with ATOM-style 32-expert chunks. In serving it killed the engine at the execute timeout during the first rebalance.\n- The fix: on ROCm, `TorchDistNcclEplbCommunicator` runs the layer as ring batches. Batch d holds the sends to rank + d and the receives from rank - d, in the same d order on every rank, so a send and its receive meet in the same batch with one peer per direction.\n- Results: 4k seeded plans in the reproducer and 4k plans through `rearrange_expert_weights_inplace` (random and default-policy plans), no hang, 0 misplaced rows, ~47 ms per layer.\n- A no-op `EplbCommunicator.prepare_transfer()` hook lets the communicator create its group's communicator with a collective first, because a ring batch need not involve every rank. Other platforms keep one batch per layer.\n\n**Per-layer skip check (commit 4).** The ROCm-only check skips a placement unless the rank-load imbalance improves by 5%. It summed each rank's load over all layers before taking max/mean, so per-layer skews on different ranks cancelled out. MegaMoE's per-layer max/mean of ~1.77 (to ~1.12 after rebalancing) read as 1.063 -> 1.118, and every rebalance was skipped. Each MoE layer waits for its busiest rank, so the check now uses the sum over layers of max over the sum over layers of mean, the same quantity `log_balancedness` reports.\n\n**Not included / follow-ups.**\n- This PR keeps the profile-time dummy rearrangement. With `torch_nccl` it all-gathers one layer of expert weights, and its load all-reduce creates the EP group's RCCL communicator, which MoRI otherwise never uses. Together that costs ~12 GiB of KV cache per rank here: 95.7 -> 83.3 GiB with EPLB on, vs 93.8 GiB in the lab with that step skipped. This can follow separately.\n- `pynccl` transfers are not ring-ordered.\n- No redundant experts.\n\nAI assistance (Claude) was used to write this change, its tests and this description; the measurements come from the author's runs.\n\n---\n\n<details>\n<summary> Pull Request Checklist </summary>\n\n- [ ] I used vLLM's `/pr-checklist` skill. Warm each point with one short request per rank, then run two formal repeats. The final DeepGEMM M1024 point uses three repeats.\r\n- For each repeat, measure the common steady-decode interval from the latest first-token timestamp to the earliest request-completion timestamp. Count streamed output tokens in that interval and report aggregate throughput across all eight ranks. all other serving flags are identical.\r\n\r\n### Routing-imbalance sensitivity\r\n\r\nThe imbalance factor `x` is the busiest destination rank's received routed slots divided by the mean received slots across the eight destination ranks:\r\n\r\n```text\r\nx = max(slots_received_by_rank) / mean(slots_received_by_rank)\r\n```\r\n\r\nThus, `1x` is perfectly balanced, while `2x`, `4x`, and `7x` mean that the busiest rank receives 2, 4, or 7 times the average routed slots. \r\n| Destinatio", "created_at": "2026-08-31T07:05:50Z", "updated_at": "2026-10-09T09:56:03Z", "fetched_at": "2026-10-19T20:56:08Z", "state": "closed", "merged_at": null, "parent": null, "tags": ["deepseek", "moe", "mla", "ttft", "throughput", "performance", "kv-cache", "nvfp4"], "metadata": {"number": 54520, "labels": [{"id": 9988761457, "node_id": "LA_kwDOI7xefs8AAAACU2BncQ", "url": "https://api.github.com/repos/vllm-project/vllm/labels/quantization", "name": "quantization", "color": "62bfab", "default": false, "description": "", "archived_at": null, "archived_by": null}], "comments": 4, "state_reason": null}, "content_hash": "565f0bb6d8f09da559b6713f980ff68e542478582a708de8e44f0979a67b454b", "rank": -20.44677454901171, "excerpt_truncated": true}, {"id": "github:vllm-project/vllm:55225", "source": "github", "kind": "pr", "url": "https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55225", "title": "[MoE] Optimize fused MoE token alignment and adaptive block size for fine-grained experts", "body": "## Summary\r\n\r\nFine-grained Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) architectures with high expert counts (such as Gemma 4 26B-A4B with E=128, top-k=8) experience severe fixed kernel launch overhead and padding inflation in vLLM's Triton fused MoE path during small-batch decode (M <= 4).\r\n\r\nThis PR introduces dynamic token alignment bypass when expert token collision is negligible (`M * topk * 4 <= E`), clamps naive block size to 16, and provides adaptive default tile sizing for fine-grained MoEs (E >= 128). Tested on upstream HEAD with an 8x NVIDIA H100 platform, this achieves up to a **1.50x speedup** on small-batch MoE dispatches (saving 35-46 µs per dispatch, ~1.05-1.38 ms per generated decode token across 30 layers) with zero loss in numerical accuracy.\r\n\r\n---\r\n\r\n## Purpose\r\n\r\n- **Problem Statement**:\r\n  1. **Fixed Kernel Launch Overhead**: In vLLM's Triton fused MoE implementation, `ops.moe_align_block_size` incurs a fixed ~20-27 µs CUDA kernel launch and prefix-sum latency per dispatch. Across the two GEMM dispatches (W13 and W2) in 30 decoder layers, this accounts for ~1.2-1.6 ms of invariant latency per generated decode token.\r\n  2. **Padding Inflation**: The default `BLOCK_SIZE_M = 64` pads token-to-expert assignments up to multiples of 64. At M=1 (8 active tokens for top-k=8), this produces 512 padded tokens—a **64.00x padding overhead** where **98.4% of processed rows are dummy padding**.\r\n  3. **Sorting Redundancy at Low Sparsity**: When `(M * topk) / E <= 0.25` (i.e. `M * topk * 4 <= E`), tokens are so sparsely distributed across experts that token collision on any individual expert is near zero (<= 1 token per expert). Under this regime, sorting and grouping tokens yields zero packing benefit while paying the full 20-27 µs alignment latency penalty.\r\n\r\n- **Changes Made**:\r\n  1. **Dynamic Token Alignment Bypass** (`vllm/model_executor/layers/fused_moe/fused_moe.py`):\r\n     - Dynamically evaluates `M * topk * 4 <= E` per forward pass.\r\n     - When satisfied, sets `sorted_token_ids=None` to engage Triton's naive block assignment path, bypassing `ops.moe_align_block_size` entirely.\r\n     - Enforces `BLOCK_SIZE_M = min(config.get(\"BLOCK_SIZE_M\", 16), 16)` in naive mode to prevent redundant computation over dummy padding rows.\r\n     - Adds full support for `expert_map` in the naive path via `expert_map[topk_ids.view(-1)]`.\r\n     - Guards against quantized WNA16 formats which do not support naive assignment.\r\n     - Routes `TritonWNA16Experts.forward()` through `_prepare_expert_assignment` (`vllm/model_executor/layers/fused_moe/experts/triton_moe.py`).\r\n  2. **Adaptive Default Tile Sizes for Fine-Grained MoEs (E >= 128)**:\r\n     - In `get_default_config()`, sets `BLOCK_SIZE_M` adaptively based on batch size: M <= 64 -> 16, M <= 256 -> 32, M > 256 -> 64.\r\n     - Corrects the `tokens_per_expert` calculation to include top-k: `(M * topk) // max(E, 1)`.\r\n  3. **Backwards-Compatible `moe_sum` Dispatcher** (`vllm/_custom_ops.py`):\r\n     - Added dual 2-arg / 4-arg fallback to support differing C++ extension build signatures seamlessly.\r\n  4. **Documentation**:\r\n     - Internal kernel execution optimization; no public API changes or user-facing doc updates required.\r\n\r\n---\r\n\r\n## Test Plan\r\n\r\nThe following test suite verifies functional correctness, bit-for-bit numerical equivalence, regression freedom across existing alignment pathways, and end-to-end model generation:\r\n\r\n1. **Unit Tests (Fine-Grained MoE & Crossover Logic)**:\r\n   ```bash\r\n   pytest tests/kernels/moe/test_fused_moe_fine_grained.py\r\n   ```\r\n   Verifies:\r\n   - Bit-for-bit numerical equivalence (`atol=0.0`) between naive bypass and aligned path.\r\n   - Crossover boundary switching behavior from naive (M=4) to aligned (M=5) for E=128, top-k=8.\r\n   - Dynamic block size selection and clamping behavior.\r\n   - Custom `expert_map` index mapping in naive mode.\r\n\r\n2. **Regression Suite (Existing MoE Alignment)**:\r\n   ```bash\r\n   pytest tests/kernels/moe/test_moe_align_block_size.py\r\n   ```\r\n\r\n3. **Code Quality & Linters**:\r\n   ```bash\r\n   ruff check vllm/model_executor/layers/fused_moe/ tests/kernels/moe/test_fused_moe_fine_grained.py vllm/_custom_ops.py\r\n   ruff format --check vllm/model_executor/layers/fused_moe/ tests/kernels/moe/test_fused_moe_fine_grained.py vllm/_custom_ops.py\r\n   ```\r\n\r\n4. **Kernel & Model Benchmarks**:\r\n   ```bash\r\n   # Crossover boundary benchmark across batch sizes\r\n   python scratch/crossover_benchmark.py\r\n\r\n   # End-to-end model generation benchmarks\r\n   python scratch/benchmark_generation_moe_models.py\r\n   ```\r\n\r\n---\r\n\r\n## Test Result\r\n\r\nAll tests and benchmarks were executed on an 8x NVIDIA H100 80GB HBM3 (SXM5) platform on current upstream HEAD.\r\n\r\n### 1. This is an opt-in admission-side approach to prefix-cache\r\nreuse loss under constrained KV capacity. It does not implement the issue's\r\nproposed waiting-queue-informed eviction policy; block eviction remains unchanged.\r\n\r\nBefore allocating blocks for a fresh waiting request, the risk policy inspects\r\nthe free-block queue against estimated full-prompt block demand, excluding the\r\ncandidate's own cache-hit blocks. Potential cached-block exposure triggers\r\nranking of a bounded waiting window by cached-prefix length.\r\n\r\n- Skip policy lookups when no admission slot is available, including slots held\r\n  by paused streaming sessions.\r\n- Recompute a promoted head's cache hit and allocation demand before allocation.\r\n- Allow at most one risk-ranking attempt per admission position.\r\n- Bound free-queue inspection; an inconclusive scan does not reject admission.\r\n\r\nThe window defaults to zero, leaving the policy disabled. The risk path currently\r\nuses the custom scheduler entry point; selecting a nonzero window alone on the\r\nbase scheduler enables the threshold-based path instead. For the risk path, set:\r\n\r\n```python\r\nscheduler_cls=\"vllm.v1.core.sched.qwenfuse_risk.RiskPerfScheduler\"\r\ncache_aware_admission_window=16\r\nenable_prefix_caching=True\r\nasync_scheduling=False\r\n```\r\n\r\nThe supported risk-policy scope is synchronous FCFS with local prefix caching\r\nand a single full-attention KV group. KV connectors, LoRA, encoder-decoder\r\nmodels, speculative/lookahead decoding, and prefix replay are excluded by the\r\ncurrent guard. The risk subclass uses exposure, rather than the configured\r\nlegacy usage threshold, as its ranking trigger.\r\n\r\n## Test Plan and Current Results\r\n\r\nValidated after integrating upstream main at\r\n`e5f3d08d722870a5301560fad0cfa65b7e333900`. The update adapts risk admission\r\nto the upstream KV-holding waiting queue and deferred-request handling.\r\nIt retains upstream queue priority, avoids popping an admitted request twice,\r\nand resets the bounded ranking retry when the admission position advances.\r\nThe change relative to that main snapshot remains confined to nine scheduler,\r\nconfiguration, KV-manager, and test files.\r\n\r\n```bash\r\n.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/v1/core/test_scheduler.py -q --tb=short\r\n.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/v1/core/test_scheduler.py   -k 'cache_aware_admission or risk_admission' -q --tb=short\r\n```\r\n\r\n- Targeted admission regression: **25 passed**. Three added tests cover\r\n  KV-holding request priority, risk rechecking after a successful admission,\r\n  and risk rechecking after a promoted request is skipped.\r\n- Full scheduler regression: **220 passed, 2 failed**. Both failures reproduce\r\n  on unmodified target main in the same single-GPU environment:\r\n  `test_async_scheduling_pp_allows_rescheduling_with_output_placeholders`\r\n  and `test_diffusion_read_deferral_keeps_a_longer_pp_wait` fail GPU-count\r\n  validation for PP=2 and PP=3 respectively. They were not skipped or\r\n  reported as passing.\r\n- Local style and type checks: **passed** on all nine changed Python files, using the applicable upstream hooks, including Ruff, typos, mypy for Python 3.10-3.13, SPDX headers, and Buildkite test tethering.\r\n- Qwen3-8B BF16 / FlashAttention 2 eager smoke passed with the risk policy,\r\n  APC, chunked prefill, window 4, and a 4 GiB KV budget. One warmup request\r\n  and two subsequent requests completed with 8 output tokens each;\r\n  the repeated prompt reused **224 cached tokens**.\r\n\r\nThis validation used the updated Python source with the existing Python\r\n3.12.14 / PyTorch 2.13.0+cu130 environment and September 20 native extensions\r\non an A100-PCIE-40GB. It is not a fresh native build or new performance result.\r\nThe eager smoke does not establish CUDA Graph execution, model-quality\r\nequivalence, or multi-GPU compatibility.\r\n\r\n## Historical Performance Results\r\n\r\nMeasurements on commit `27757dde`, A100-PCIE-40GB, Qwen3-8B BF16,\r\nFlashAttention 2, with APC and chunked prefill enabled. Baseline and risk-policy\r\nruns used the same operator configuration; only the admission policy differed.\r\n\r\nFor the constrained 8 GiB random mixed fixed-burst workload:\r\n\r\n- Median throughput improvement: **5.02%**.\r\n- Median batch completion time reduction: **4.78%**.\r\n- Five of six paired comparisons improved; throughput changes ranged from\r\n  **-2.37% to +6.12%**.\r\n- Sufficient-capacity and all-cold controls showed near-zero median changes.\r\n- Cold-request TTFT increased substantially for one seed.\r\n\r\nThese are historical fixed-burst measurements, not sustained-serving results\r\nor performance measurements on the current submitted head.\r\n\r\n## Known Limits and Review Questions\r\n\r\n- Cached-prefix ranking can delay cold requests; the current implementation\r\n  does not provide an aging mechanism or a bound on waiting time.\r\n- A bounded waiting window cannot discover reusable prefixes beyond that window.\r\n- Exposure is a snapshot-based estimate, not proof that a particular waiting\r\n  request's reusable prefix will be evicted. The eviction turns a would-be cache\nhit into recomputation, and the deeper the shared prefix, the more expensive the loss.\nLooking a bounded distance ahead lets the scheduler admit the resident request first, so\nthe cold request's allocation no longer destroys reuse that was about to be collected.\n\nTwo new scheduler options, both off by default:\n\n| Flag | Default | Meaning |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| `--cache-aware-admission-window N` | 0 | Look-ahead depth over the waiting queue; 0 admits strictly in arrival order |\n| `--cache-aware-admission-threshold F` | 0.0 | KV cache usage below which the window is not applied |\n\nDeliberate limits on scope:\n\n- Reordering is confined to the window, and within it to the slots held by plain\n  `WAITING` requests with nothing computed. A request in a blocked status\n  (`WAITING_FOR_REMOTE_KVS` and friends) or one already partially computed keeps its exact\n  position, and nothing is moved past it. This is a reordering among peers, not a change\n  in priority.\n- It requires the `fcfs` policy. Positional reordering is meaningless in a priority heap —\n  `PriorityRequestQueue.prepend_request` re-inserts by priority and ignores position — and\n  it would also fight the user's explicit priorities. Under `priority` the feature is\n  disabled at construction.\n- It requires prefix caching, and is skipped on steps that defer prefill anyway.\n- The threshold exists so operators can confine reordering to periods where there are\n  evictions worth avoiding. It defaults to 0.0 (always apply) so that setting only the\n  window has a visible effect rather than silently doing nothing on a roomy cache.\n\nScoring uses a new `KVCacheManager.get_num_cached_tokens`, which probes the cache without\ntaking references, allocating blocks, or emitting events, so it is safe to call for a\nrequest that may not be scheduled. `get_computed_blocks` is unsuitable for this: it emits\n`BlockStored` events under `kv_cache_report_mode=\"full\"`, which would publish phantom\nevents for requests that were only scored.\n\nCost is one prefix-cache lookup per request in the window per step, plus one linear pass\nover the waiting queue when the order actually changes.\n\n## Why this is not duplicating an existing PR\n\n**#53571** (SLO-aware scheduling policy) is the closest existing work and deserves an\nexplicit comparison, since it also reorders waiting requests. It is keyed on TTFT targets\nand prompt length, to stop short prompts queueing behind long prefills; this is keyed on\nresident prefix length, to stop cold admissions evicting reusable blocks. Different\nsignal, different objective, and they could coexist.\n\nThe implementations diverge deliberately. #53571 adds a new `SchedulingPolicy` value,\nwhich makes it mutually exclusive with `priority`. This is a bounded per-step re-sort\nbehind its own flag, so it composes with whatever policy is in force rather than replacing\nit. If maintainers would prefer these unified under one policy mechanism, I am glad to\nadapt.\n\nOther adjacent open PRs, none overlapping:\n\n- **#54327** (bounded prefix-aware filesystem cache eviction) concerns the KV-offload\n  filesystem tier, not admission order.\n- **#53772** and **#54348** rework promotion of blocked `skipped_waiting` requests. This\n  PR pins non-`WAITING` requests in place, so it composes; the status filter is covered by\n  the tests.\n- **#45558** (priority-based preemption at `max_num_seqs`) preempts running requests; this\n  never preempts.\n\nChecks run (`gh` was not authenticated in my environment, so these were run against the\npublic search API; the equivalent `gh` invocations are):\n\n```bash\ngh pr list --repo vllm-project/vllm --state open --search \"cache-aware admission\"\ngh pr list --repo vllm-project/vllm --state open --search \"prefix cache admission order\"\ngh pr list --repo vllm-project/vllm --state open --search \"waiting queue reorder\"\n```\n\n## Test Plan\n\nFive new tests in `tests/v1/core/test_scheduler.py`, built on a helper that seeds the\nprefix cache with one request's prompt so a later request is genuinely warm:\n\n- `test_cache_aware_admission_prefers_cached_prefix` — the warm request is admitted ahead\n  of the cold one that arrived first.\n- `test_cache_aware_admission_does_not_look_past_the_window` — a warm request outside the\n  window keeps its place in arrival order.\n- `test_cache_aware_admission_skipped_below_threshold` — below the usage threshold,\n  admission stays in arrival order.\n- `test_cache_aware_admission_requires_fcfs_and_prefix_caching[priority, no-caching]` —\n  the feature is off under the `priority` policy and with prefix caching disabled.\n\n`create_scheduler` gains `cache_aware_admission_window`, `cache_aware_admission_threshold`\nand `policy` parameters.\n\n## Test Result\n\nAll green. "github:vllm-project/vllm:57658", "source": "github", "kind": "pr", "url": "https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57658", "title": "[Spec Decode] Fix Hybrid Mamba KV-cache admission for dynamic K", "body": "## Summary\r\n\r\nFix a correctness issue in Hybrid Mamba KV-cache admission when the existing scheduler selects a dynamic speculative-token count (`K`) based on the current batch size.\r\n\r\nThe scheduler already supports dynamic `K`, but Mamba state admission was still accounted using the maximum `K`. This over-reserved KV-cache capacity and caused avoidable request queuing under concurrent workloads.\r\n\r\nThis PR makes admission, allocation, lookahead allocation, and `SchedulerOutput` use the same step-level `K` selected by the scheduler. It does not introduce a new dynamic-`K` policy.\r\n\r\n## Motivation\r\n\r\nBefore this fix, the scheduler could select a smaller `K` for larger batches, while Mamba admission continued to reserve state for the maximum `K`. The resulting accounting mismatch reduced the number of requests admitted per iteration and created unnecessary waiting, especially at concurrency 16 and 32.\r\n\r\n## Changes\r\n\r\n- Use the scheduler's current step-level `K` for Mamba KV-cache admission.\r\n- Propagate the same `K` through allocation and lookahead allocation.\r\n- Keep `SchedulerOutput` consistent with the admission decision.\r\n- Preserve the existing dynamic-`K` selection policy.\r\n- Add focused unit tests for Mamba admission accounting and scheduler dynamic-`K` consistency.\r\n\r\n## Validation\r\n\r\n### Unit tests\r\n\r\n`8 passed`\r\n\r\nThe tests cover:\r\n\r\n- Mamba admission block accounting.\r\n- Consistency of dynamic `K` across the scheduler, allocation, lookahead allocation, and `SchedulerOutput`.\r\n\r\nThese tests validate correctness invariants. The end-to-end benchmark below measures the resulting scheduler behavior and throughput.\r\n\r\n### End-to-End Throughput\r\n\r\nBoth runs used the same protocol:\r\n\r\n- Qwen3.5-4B with DFlash\r\n- Input/output length: 1024/256\r\n- Closed-loop streaming workload\r\n- `settle=first C completions`\r\n- `N=max(256, 8C)`\r\n- `max_num_batched_tokens=16384`\r\n- `max_num_seqs=128`\r\n- Prefix caching disabled\r\n- Eager execution enabled\r\n- Seed 42\r\n\r\n| Concurrency | Before (tokens/s) | After (tokens/s) | Improvement |\r\n| ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |\r\n| 16 | 414.76 | 752.60 | +81.5% |\r\n| 32 | 392.23 | 950.20 | +142.3% |\r\n\r\nRequest throughput improved from 1.6202 to 2.9399 req/s at concurrency 16, and from 1.5321 to 3.7117 req/s at concurrency 32.\r\n\r\n### Steady-State Scheduler Capacity\r\n\r\nThe following values are summarized from 10-second server-metric samples after each concurrency point completed its settling phase. Startup ramp-up samples and final drain samples are excluded. MambaManager admission", "body": "## Purpose\n\nFix the wrong `num_new_blocks` calculation in `MambaManager.get_num_blocks_to_allocate` (align mode) when a KV connector cache hit is loaded synchronously on a hybrid model with Mamba/GDN layers (`--mamba-cache-mode align`). The estimate is one block short per Mamba group, so the request is admitted with `free == estimate` and the pool raises inside the allocation, killing the EngineCore:\n\n```\nFile \"vllm/v1/core/kv_cache_coordinator.py\", line 299, in allocate_new_blocks\nFile \"vllm/v1/core/single_type_kv_cache_manager.py\", line 1882, in allocate_new_blocks\n    new_blocks = self.block_pool.get_new_blocks(num_new_blocks)\nFile \"vllm/v1/core/block_pool.py\", line 681, in get_new_blocks\nValueError: Cannot get 1 free blocks from the pool\n```\n\nCause: the align-mode estimate caps `num_new_blocks` at what `allocate_new_blocks` pulls, but when external computed tokens and new tokens are allocated in the same `allocate_slots` call, the inherited `allocate_external_computed_blocks` pulls the boundary state block first, and that block is not counted. Async loads are unaffected (the external span is allocated with no new tokens) and #57050 only fixed the over-estimate in the same function, so this is still present on `main`.\n\nFix: count the blocks `allocate_external_computed_blocks` will pull (`cdiv(total_computed, block_size) - max(skipped, held)`) and size the `allocate_new_blocks` share from the blocks held after it. The estimate then equals the allocation for sync loads and stays exact for async loads and cold prefills.\n\n### Supersedes #46892\n\n#46892 (thanks @zqs164) found the same bug and was approved by @MengqingCao and @ivanium in June, but pre-commit failed, it has needed a rebase since #57050 rewrote the cap in July, and there has been no activity on it since — the bug is still live on `main` three months later and kills the engine whenever a KV connector loads a cache hit synchronously on a Mamba/GDN hybrid in align mode, which is why we are opening a new PR rather than waiting on it. It also takes a different approach rather than rebasing #46892:\n\n- #46892 adds a constant `+1` inside the cap. That is exact only when the claim ends on a block boundary; for a mid-block claim (e.g. 133 external + 11 new tokens with block size 16) it reserves a block that is never pulled, one per Mamba group, which can refuse a request that fits near the pool ceiling. This change counts the actual pull, so admission equals allocation in both cases (the invariant `test_mamba_checkpoint_admission_matches_allocation` asserts).\n- The external block is counted outside `physical_block_cap`, which only describes `allocate_new_blocks`, so the fix does not depend on the cap's formula (the coupling that made #46892 conflict with #57050).\n- \"External tokens present\" is tested with `total_computed_tokens > num_local_computed_tokens` instead of `len(new_computed_blocks) * block_size`, which also holds under partial local hits.\n\nThe `request_id not in self.num_cached_block` gate suggested in #46892's review is kept.\n\n## Test Plan\n\n```\npytest tests/v1/core/test_single_type_kv_cache_manager.py -k mamba\npytest tests/v1/core/test_prefix_caching.py -k \"external_claim or mamba_align\"\n```\n\n- `test_mamba_external_claim_admission_matches_allocation` replays the coordinator's order (`add_local_computed_blocks` -> `allocate_external_computed_blocks` -> `allocate_new_blocks`) for a cold prefill and aligned, non-aligned and async claims, each followed by the next chunk, asserting `estimate == allocated`.\n- `test_mamba_align_external_claim_admission_matches_allocation` does the same through `KVCacheManager.allocate_slots` on a `full + mamba_align` layout.\n\nWithout the fix the sync-claim cases fail (`assert 1 == 2`).\n\nEnd-to-end: Qwen3.8-27B-FP8 (3 GDN groups) TP1 on H100 with a KV connector doing synchronous loads, `--num-gpu-blocks-override 28 --max-model-len 32768 --max-num-seqs 8`;
